At whose bidding we offer holy oil to God as a type of his mercifulness and gladness, in which also the divine and glad light of grace flashes forth. For we offer lights also.

And since the prayer too is offered, and the oil is sanctified, those who are anointed with the oil find the remission of sins, as the harlot too, having anointed the saving feet, was herself anointed from thence.

CHAPTER 57. Why there is oil both in the holy and great myron, and oil in the oil of prayer.

And how is there oil both in the divine and greatest myron, which bears the seal of the Spirit, and oil in the oil of those who repent and of the sick? Because both there and here the oil has a type of the divine mercy. For there, in the myron, it signifies salvation for us, and the redemption from death, and the seal of the life-giving and glad Spirit, through whom also we were delivered from the flood of sin. Wherefore also in the flood in the time of Noah a dove, as a type of the Spirit, carrying a twig of olive in its mouth, *74* made salvation plain. And in the flood of sin, too, at the baptism of the Lord the Spirit came down in the form of a dove, bearing witness to the divine mercy and to the grace. And here too, as a type of the divine — as we have said — mercifulness and compassion toward us, this oil, being offered, is sanctified, and through it we draw the mercy of God upon ourselves.

Ἰκανὰ ταῦτα πρὸς ἀπόδειξιν τῶν τῆς Ἐκκλησίας ἱερῆν μυστηρίων, ἢ καὶ πλειόνων ἔχετε χρείαν; 208ζΑΡΟΤ Γviii. Κληρικός. Ἰκανὰ, δέσποτα, καὶ οὐ χρείαν ἔ ορε ἄλλων. Πλὴν δεόμεθά οου, ἕκαστν ἐκθεῖναι τῶν μυστηρίων, καὶ τὰ ἱερὰ ἡμῖν ἀναπτύξαι σύμβολα, καὶ διδάξαι ἡμᾶς τίς ἑκάστου τυγχάνει τῶν τελουμένων ἡ δύναμις, Ἀρχιερεύς. Ὑπὲρ ἡμᾶς, ὡς εἰρήκαμεν, τοῦτο, ἀδελφὲ, καὶ δυνάμεως, καὶ σοφίας ὑψηλοτέρας. Εἰ γὰρ Διονὔσιός τε καὶ Μάξιμος μεθ´ ὑποστολῆς τε καὶ εὐλεβείας ὅσης οὐκ ἔστιν εἰπεῖν, ἐγχειρεῖν ἐπεχείρουν τοῖς τοιούτοις ἱεροῖς πράγμασι, πῶς ἡμῖν ἔσται δυνα-λι ἄρα περὶ τοιούτων ἐφερμηνεύειν; Ἀλλ´ ἐπειδήπερ τὸ θαῤῥεῖν εἰς Θεὸν πάντα δύναται, αὐτὸς γὰρ τοῖς πιστεύουσι παρέχει κατὰ τὴν πίστιν, καὶ διὰ τὴν ἀγάπην δὲ χορηγεῖ, ὅτι καὶ ἀγάπη ἐστί· λοιπὸν καὶ παεῖς διὰ τὴν ὑμετέραν λέγοντες ἀγάπην, πιστειὶς μεν λαβεῖν λόγον ἐν ἀνοίξει τοῦ στόματος ἡμῶν. Καὶ ἰδοὺ ἐγχειροῦμεν δι´ ὑπακοὴν ἐν ἀγάπη Χριτῶ ἐκτελέσαι τὴν ὑμετέραν αἴτησιν. Καὶ περὶ ἑκάστον τῶν ἱερῶν συμβόλων ὅπερ ἔγνωμεν ἐκ τῶν ἱερωτάτων Γραφῶν ἐροῦμεν. Καὶ ὅπερ ἄν ἡμᾶς οἱ Πατέρες ἡμῶν ἐδίδαξαν λέξομεν, καθόσον ἐξισχύσαμεν γνῶναι. Καὶ δὴ πρῶτον λέγομεν περὶ τοῦ θείου Βαπτίσματος · ἐπεὶ καὶ τοῦτο ἀρχὴ τῶν εἰς ἡμᾶς μυστηΡἰων Πλὴν ὅτι καὶ ποὸ τοῦ βαπτισθῆναι σαρκικὴ γέννησις ἡμῖν γίνεται, ῥητέον καὶ περὶ τῆς ἐν ταύτῃ πρότερον τελουμένης εὐχῆς. Ἐπεὶ γὰρ γεγεννημένοι σαρκὶ, οὕτως εἰς τὴν ἀναγέννησιν τὴν διὰ τῶ Πνεύματος ὁδηγούμεθα, ἡ Ἐκκλησία τοῦ Χριστου, ὡς ἄσπιλος οὖσα καὶ ἡγιασμένη ἐν Χριστῷ, ἐν τοῖς ἔὺσεβέσιν εὐθὺς τὴν ἁμαρτωλὸν ἡμμῶν καὶ ἐκ σαρκὸς γέννησιν ἐκκαθαίρει. ΚΕΦΑΛ. ΝΗʹ. Διατί ἐν τῇ τοῦ βμέφους γεννήσει εὐχὴ πορὰ τρῦ Ιερέως γίνεται. Καὶ τεχθέντος τοῦ βρέφους παρὰ γυναικὸς εὐσεβοῦς, ὁ ἱερεὺς παραγίνεται, καὶ δωξολογεῖ τὸν Θεὸν, εὐχαριστῶν, ὅτι ἄνθρωπος γεγέννηται ἐν τῷ κόσμῳ· καὶ σφραγίσας, εὐλογεῖ τὸ τεχθὲν, καὶ διαμένειν καὶ δέξασθαι τὸ βάπτισμα καὶ τὸ χρίσμα αἰτεῖται. Τῇ μητρί τε τὰ πρὸς σωτηρίαν εὑχόμενος, μεταδίδωσιν αὐτῇ τε καὶ ταῖς σὺν αὐτῇ γυναιξὶ χάριτος καὶ ἁγιασμοῦ, ἄδειάν τε παρέχει ἐνεργεῖν τὰ ἔργα αὐτῶν, καὶ κατὰ μηδὲν ὅλως κωλύεσθαι, ἢ ῥύπιυ μετέχειν, ἢ ἐνδοιάζειν φθονεροῖς τοῦ πονηροῦ φάσμασιν, ἐπειδὴ τῷ τῆς ἁμαρτίας καὶ ἐξ ἡδονῆς ἐξυπηρέτησαν τόκῳ, ὃν δὴ τῆς φθορᾶς καὶ τοῦ θανάτου προοίμιόν τινές φασι καὶ ἔστιν. ΚΕΦΙΛ. ΝΘʹ. ήλωσις κατὰ μέρος τῶν πρὸ τοῦ βαπτισματος, καὶ ἐν τῷ βαπτίσματι, καὶ μετὰ τὸ βάπτιιμα τελουμένων Ἀλλὰ καὶ σφραγίσας ὕδωρ τῇ σημειώσει τοῦ σταυροῦ, εἰς προοίμιον τοῦ θείου βαπτίσματος καταῤῥαντίζει τὸν οἶκον.

Are these things sufficient for the demonstration of the sacred mysteries of the Church, or have you need of more also?

208Cleric. They are sufficient, master, and we have no need of others. But we beseech thee to set forth each of the mysteries, and to unfold to us the sacred symbols, and to teach us what the power of each of the things celebrated is.

Bishop. This is beyond us, as we have said, brother, and belongs to a power and a wisdom more lofty. For if Dionysius and Maximus, with a reserve and a reverence so great as cannot be told, undertook to set their hand to such sacred matters, how then will it be possible for us to interpret such things? But since confidence toward God can do all things — for he himself affords to those who believe according to their faith, and supplies also on account of love, because he is also love — for the rest we too, speaking on account of your love, believe that we shall receive a word in the opening of our mouth. And behold, through obedience, in the love of Christ, we set our hand to accomplish your request. And concerning each of the sacred symbols we will speak what we have learned from the most sacred Scriptures. And whatever our Fathers taught us we will tell, so far as we have had strength to know. And first indeed we speak concerning the divine baptism; since this too is the beginning of the mysteries that come to us. But because a fleshly birth comes to us even before being baptized, we must speak also of the prayer that is celebrated first at it. For since, having been born in the flesh, we are thus guided to the regeneration that is through the Spirit, the Church of Christ, as being spotless and sanctified in Christ, at once cleanses among the pious our sinful birth, which is from the flesh.

CHAPTER 58. Why at the birth of the infant a prayer is made by the priest.

And when the infant has been born of a pious woman, the priest comes, and glorifies God, giving thanks that a man has been born into the world; and having sealed it, he blesses the child that was born, and asks that it may continue, and receive baptism and the chrism. And praying for the mother the things that are for salvation, he imparts to her and to the women with her grace and sanctification, and grants leave to do their works [the Latin gives facultatem praebet sanctificare opera, grants leave to sanctify works], and in nothing at all to be hindered, or to partake of defilement, or to waver at the envious phantasms of the evil one, since they have ministered to the birth that is of sin and from pleasure, which indeed some say is a prelude of corruption and of death, and so it is.

CHAPTER 59. A setting-forth, part by part, of the things celebrated before baptism, and in baptism, and after baptism.

But having also sealed water with the marking of the cross, as a prelude of the divine baptism he sprinkles the house.

Κατασφραγίσας δὲ καὶ τὸ βρέφος τῷ μετώπῳ διὰ τὸν νοῦν, καὶ τῷ στόματι δὲὰ τὸν λόγον καὶ τὴν πνοὴν. καὶ τῇ καρδίᾳ διὰ τὴν ζωτικὴν δύναμιν, ὥστε μένειν πεφυλαγμένον εἰς τὸ 209σωτήριον βάπτισμα, οὕτως ἀπολύσας, Ξἀπέρχεται. Τῇ δὲ ὀγδόῃ ἡμέρᾳ προσάγεται τὸ βρέφος ὑπό τινων τῷ Θεῷ, καὶ πὸ τῶν πυλῶν, οὐ γὰρ ἡγιασμένον ἔτι ἐστὶ τῷ βαπτίσματι, σφραγίζει πάλιν αὐτὸ εἰς τὸ μέτωπον, καὶ τὸ στόμα, καὶ τὸ στῆθος ὁ ἱερεύς· καὶ δίζωσιν ὄνομα τούτῳ δι´ εὐχῆς ἱερᾶς, ὅπερ ἂν οἱ τεκόντες θελήσαιεν, μεθ´ οὖ καὶ βαπτίνεται ΚΕΦΑΛ. Σʹ. ρτι καὶ ἐν τῇ ὀγδόῃ ἡμέρᾳ ὁ Κύριος τὸ ὄνομα, εληφεν, Ἰησοῦς κληθείςΚαθάπερ τοῦτο γέγονε καὶ ἐν τῷ Κυρίῳ· ἐν τῇ ὀγδόῃ γὰρ περιτμηθεὶς, Ἰησοῦς ἐπεκλήθη. Ἡμεῖς δὲ οὐ περιτομήν ἔχομεν· πεπλήρωκε γὰρ ὁ Σωτὴρ τὰ τοῦ νόμου ἀντὶ ἡμῶν, καὶ τῆς νομικῆς δουλείας, ἐρύσατο. Δέδωκε δὲ περιτομὴν ἄλλην ἑν ὅλῃ τῇ φύσει, τὸ θεῖον βάπτισμα, ὅπερ οὐ σάρκα τέμνει, ἀλλ´ ἀμαρτίας καθαίρει. Ἔκαστον δὲ τῶν βρεφῶν ὡς Ἰησοῦς ὁ Χριστὸς ἐν τῷ ἰδίῳ βαπτίζεται ὀνόματι. Οὐ μὴν δὲ βαπτίζονται πάντες Ἰωάννης καὶ Μαρία, ώς τῶν ἁπλουστέρων καὶ ἰδιωτικῶν τινές φασιν. Ἐν τῇ ὀγδόῃ δὲ ἡ περιτομὴ τότε ἦν τοῖς Ἰουδαίοις. Τὸ ὄνομα δὲ νῦν ἐν αὐτῇ οἱ πιστοὶ λαμβάνομεν, ὅτι ἀνακαινισμὸς ἡ ὀγδόη. Πληρωθείσης γὰρι τῆς ἑβδόμης ἣν ἡ ζωὴ αὕτη κέκτηται, δι´ ἐπτὰ κυκλουμένη τῶν ἡμερῶν, τῇ ὀγδόῃ πάλιν ἀπάρχεται. σημεῖον τῆς ἀναστάσεώς ἐστι, καὶ τῆς αἰωνίου ζωῆς προοίμιον. Ἐν γὰρ τῇ ὁγδόη καὶ ὁ Κύριος ἐξανέστη, καὶ τὴν τελευταίαν ἡμεῖς ἡμέραν τῆς ανωνίου ζωῆς ἐλπίζομεν. Ἥτις καὶ ἀπέραντος ἔσται, καθὰ διῦασκόμεθα. Ἐν ταύτῃ οὖν τῇ ὀγδόῃ οἱ μὲν ἱα δαῖοι περιετέμνοντο κατὰ τὰ ἄῤῤενα μόνον ὡς ἀτελεῖς, σφραγιζόμενοι τῷ μορίῳ τῆς ἡδονῆς, προμαρτυροῦντες μὲν καὶ αὐτοὶ τὴν ἀνάστασιν τῇ ῥνδόῃ, καὶ ἐν τῷ περιτέμνεσθαι δέ, σημαίνοντες ὡς ἡ ἡδονὴ τῆς σαρκὸς παυθήσεταί ποτε, καὶ ἄφθαρτος ἔσται ζωή, καὶ μὴ διὰ σαρκὸς ἐνεργουμένη, μὴ νοῦντες δὲ τοῦτο, μηδὲ τὴν ἀληθῆ περιτομὴν τὴν τῆς ἁμαρτίας κατανοοῦντες ἀπόθεσιν, ὅπερ ἐστὶ τὸ ἄγιον βάπτισμα. Τοῦτο γὰρ τὸ ἀφθάρτους ὅλους εʹναι καὶ ἀσινεῖς ἐκδιδάσκει, καὶ μὴ κατά τι λελωβημένους · ὁ γὰρ περιτεμνόμενος τὴν σάρκα λελῷ. βηται, καὶ τὸ ἄρτιον οὐ περιφέρει τῆς φύσεως· καὶ παι οὐκ ἀπαθὲς ἐξ ἡδονῆς τίκτεσθαι ἡμᾶς. Διὸ καὶ τοῦτο λέλυται τὸ ἐξ ἡδονῆς τίκτεσθαι. Δοθήσεται δὲ ἠμῖν διὰ τοῦ βαπτίσματος τὸ οὐκ ἐκ θελήματος σαρκὸς, οὐδὲ ἐκ θελήματος ἀνδρὸς, ἀλλ´ ἐκ Θεοῦ τίκτεσθαι. Τοῦτο δ´ ἐστὶ τὸ διὰ λουτροῦ τῆς παλιγγενεσίας ἀναγεννᾶσθαι. Ὅθεν καὶ τῆ ὀγδόῃ ἡμέρᾳ τὸ ενομα λαμόάνει μὲν τὸ βρέφος, ὥσπερ ὁ Σωτὴρ τὸ Σωτήριον Ἰησοῦς ἐπικληθείς· σφραγισθὲν δὲ καὶ εὐχὴν λαβὼν ἱερὰν, ὡς προσαχθὲν τῷ Κυρίῳ, καὶ αημειωθὲν τῷ σταυρῷ τὸ μέτωπον, ὡς ἔφην, διὰ τὸ νοητικὸν, τὸ στόμα διὰ τὸ λογικὸν, καὶ τὴν καρκεαν διὰ τὸ ζωοποιὸν, καὶ τῇ βίβλῳ τῇ αἰωνίῳ γραφὲν διὰ τοῦ ἐπιτεθέντος αὐτῷ ὀνόματος, ἐπανέρχεται τῇ μητρί.

Τῇ τεσσαρακοστῇ δὲ ἡμέρᾳ πάλιν προσάγεται ὑπὸ τῆς μητρὸς τῷ ναῷ καὶ τοῦτο ὡς ἀῶρον Θεῷ προσκομίζεται.

And having sealed the infant also on the forehead, on account of the mind, and on the mouth, on account of the word and the breath, and on the heart, on account of the vital power, so that it may remain guarded for the 209saving baptism, having thus dismissed them, *75* he departs. And on the eighth day the infant is brought to God by certain persons, and before the gates — for it is not yet sanctified by baptism — the priest seals it again on the forehead, and the mouth, and the breast; and gives it a name through a sacred prayer, whatever those who bore it may wish, with which it is also baptized.

CHAPTER 60. That on the eighth day too the Lord received the name, being called Jesus.

Just as this took place in the Lord also; for on the eighth day, being circumcised, he was called Jesus. But we do not have circumcision; for the Saviour has fulfilled the things of the law on our behalf, and has rescued us from the bondage of the law. And he has given another circumcision in the whole of nature, the divine baptism, which does not cut flesh, but cleanses sins. And each of the infants is baptized in its own name, as Jesus the Christ was. Not, however, are all baptized John and Mary, as some of the simpler and unlettered folk say. On the eighth day, then, circumcision was for the Jews. But now we the faithful receive the name on it, because the eighth is a renewal. For when the seventh is fulfilled, which this life possesses, revolving through seven of the days, it begins again on the eighth. Which is a sign of the resurrection, and a prelude of eternal life. For on the eighth the Lord also rose again, and we hope for the last day, that of eternal life. Which too shall be without end, as we are taught. On this eighth, then, the Jews were circumcised as to the males only, as being imperfect, sealed on the member of pleasure, themselves too bearing witness beforehand to the resurrection by the eighth, and signifying by being circumcised that the pleasure of the flesh shall cease at some time, and life shall be incorruptible, and not wrought through the flesh; but not understanding this, nor perceiving the true circumcision, the putting-off of sin, which is the holy baptism. For this teaches the being wholly incorruptible and unharmed, and not maimed in any respect; for he who is circumcised in the flesh has been maimed, and does not carry about the entireness of nature; and further, that our being born from pleasure is not passionless. Wherefore this too has been loosed, the being born from pleasure. And there shall be given to us through baptism to be born not of the will of the flesh, nor of the will of man, but of God. And this is to be regenerated through the washing of rebirth. Whence on the eighth day too the infant receives the name, just as the Saviour was called by the saving name, Jesus; and having been sealed, and having received a sacred prayer, as one brought to the Lord, and signed with the cross on the forehead, as I said, for the intellectual part, on the mouth for the rational part, and on the heart for the life-giving part, and written in the eternal book through the name that was laid upon it, it returns to the mother.

And on the fortieth day it is brought again by the mother to the temple, and this is presented to God as a gift.

Ὁ ἱερεὺς γὰρ πρὸ τῶν θυρῶν στας τοῦ ναοῦ (ὐ γὰρ θέμις πρὸ τῆς εὐχῆς ΟἈΡΓΤ ΙΚ. 212εἰσελθεῖν) καὶ τὴν μητέρα σφραγίσας ἅμα τῷ βρέφει, καὶ ἁγιάσας αὐτοὺς εὐχαῖς, καὶ τῇ μὲν μητρὶ τὴν ἀπὸ τῆς ἐνηδόνου καὶ ῥυπώδους γεννήσεως κάθαρσιν παρασχὼν, πεπληρωκυίας μετὰ τὸν τόκον τὸ τεσσαρακονθήμερον, ἐν ᾧ καὶ τὸ βρέφος ἐν αὐτῇ ἐτελέσθη καὶ σκιρτᾷν ἤρξατο· καὶ δοὺς αὐτῇ τὴν ἑν τῷ ναῷ εἴσοδον, μέχρι τότε μὴ οὕσης ταύτης ἀξίας τοῦ εἰσελθεῖν, μηδὲ κοινωνῆσαι τοῦ καθαροῦ, τὸ παιδίον εἰς χεῖρας αὐτὸς λαβὼν, τὰ τοῦ Συμεὼν ἐκτυπῶν ἀγκαλισαμένου βρέφος τὸν Κύριον, τὸ, «Πῦν ἀπολύεις,» ἀναβοᾷ, ἐκ τῆς ἀμαρτίας λυθῆναι τὸ βρέφος ὁ ἱερεὺς εὐχόμενος, καὶ φῶς ἰδεῖν τὸν Χριστὸν, τὴν ἀποκάλυψιν τῶν ἐθνῶν καὶ δόξαν τοῦ νέου Ἰσραὴλ, ἧξ γεγεννημένον καὶ αὐτὸ Πνεύματι, καὶ Θεὸν ὁρῶν ἄντικρυς. Καὶ εἰ μὲν τὸ βρέφος βεβαπτισμένον ἐστὶν, εἰσάγει τοῦτο κἀν τῷ θυσιαστηρίῳ, καὶ κύκλῳ αὐτὸ περιάγει, ὥσπερ ποιῶν καὶ προσκύνησιν, δηλῶν ὡς ἀνάθημα τοῦτο Θεῷ, καὶ προσκυνεῖ τῷ ποιήσαντι. Εἰ δὲ μήπω βαπτισθέν ἐστι τὸ παιδίον,· πρὸ τῶν διαστύλων στὰς, καὶ διὰ τοῦ βρέφους προσκύνησιν ἐν τῷ θυσιαστηρίῳ ποιήσας, καὶ τῇ μητρὶ παραδοὺς αὐτὸ, ἀπολύει. Ἐντεῦθεν οὖν τὸ μὲν βρέφος ἐστὶ κατηχούμενος · ἡ δέ γε μήτηρ ἄδειαν εἴληφε τόν τε ναὸν ὡς καθαρισθεῖσα εἰσιέναι, καὶ τῶν μυστηρίων μετέχειν, ὁπότε ἑτοίμως ἔχει καὶ βούλεται. Καὶ ταῦτα μὲν οὕτως. ΚΕΦΑΛ. ΞΑʹ. Θεωρία περὶ τῶν τελουμένων ἐν τῷ θείῳ βαπτί ΟΗΤίι. Τὰ τοῦ ερωτάτου δὲ βαπτίσματος καταμάνθανε ἥδη· καὶ πρῶτον μὲν ὡς οὐκ ἔστι τούτου καιρὸς ἡμερῶν ἢ ὡρῶν, μή ποτε ἀφώτιστον ἀπέλθοι τὸ βρέφος. Ἀλλ´ εἰ φόβος θανάτου, πάντα εὐθὺς τῷ γεννηθῆναι τὰ κατὰ τύπον γίνεται· πλὴν μετὰ προσοχῆς, καὶ ἱερέως εὐλαβοῦς τε καὶ νήφοντος· καὶ εἴγε δίδωτιν ὁ καιρὸς, καὶ μὴ φόβος θαοάτου, μετὰ τὴν Ἰερουργίαν εὐθὺς, ἵνα καὶ μεταλάβῃ τὸ βρέφος τῶν μυστηρίων. Καὶ περὶ τούτου φησὶ Διονύσιος, ὡς πάντα τὰ μυστήρια τελειοῦσιν ἡμᾶς τῇ κοινωνίᾳ. Τοῦτο γὰρ τὸ πέρας πάντων ἐστίν. Ὁφείλει δέ γε δ ἰερεὺς καὶ τὰς ἱερὰς καλῶς μελετῆσαι καὶ λέγειν εὐχάς, καὶ μετὰ προσοχῆς, καὶ ἀργῶς, εἰ μή τις ἀνάγκη ἀναγινώσκειν ἐν ἐπηκόῳ. Ἥκουσται γὰρ ἡμῖν παρὰ Πατέρων, ὡς οἱ ὑπὸ φασμάτων δειματούμενοι πολλάκις, διὰ τοῦτο πάσχουσι τοῦτο, ὅτιπερ οἱ βαπτίσαντες ἱερεῖς οὐ μετὰ προσοχῆς τοὺς ἀφορκισμοὺς εἶπον, καὶ τὰς ἑτέρας ἰερὰς εὐχάς. Εἴπερ οὖν ἔστιν ἄδεια, οὐχ ἅπαξ μόνον χρὴ λέγειν τὰς ἀπ´ ἀρχῆς τῶν ἀφορκισμῶν, καὶ τὰς ἑτέρας εύχάε, ἀλλὰ πολλάκις. Καὶ γὰρ ἐν μὲν τῷ εὑχολογίῳ ὀκτάκις ἢ δεκάκις γράφεται λέγεσθαι, ὅτι καὶ συνήθεια ἦν ἀρχαία τῆς Ἑκκλησίας, καθ´ ἑκάστην μὲν ἐν ἑπτὰ ἡμέραις ὑπὸ τῶν κατηχητῶν ἱερέων τοῖς βαπτιζομένοις ταύτας λέγεσθαι, τῇ ὀγδόῃ δὲ πάλιν ὑπὸ τοῦ ἀρχιερέως, ἢ τοῦ μέλλοντος βαπτίσαι ἱερέως· καὶ οὕτως ἐβαπτίζοντο ὑπὸ τοῦ ἀρχιερέως ἢ ἱερέως· ἡμεῖς δὲ εἴδομεν εὐλαβεῖς ἱερεῖς τρὶς ἀπαραλείπτως λέγοντας ταύτας, καὶ οὕτω βαπειίζωντας.

For the priest, standing before the doors of the temple (for it is not lawful to 212enter before the prayer), and having sealed the mother together with the infant, and sanctified them with prayers, and having afforded to the mother the cleansing from the birth that is pleasure-laden and defiled — she having completed the forty days after the bearing, in which the infant too was perfected in her and began to leap; and having given her the entrance into the temple, she being until then not worthy to enter, nor to have communion with what is pure — himself taking the child into his arms, figuring the part of Symeon, who took the Lord, an infant, in his arms, cries aloud the «Now lettest thou depart,» the priest praying that the infant be loosed from sin, and see the light, Christ, the revelation of the Gentiles and the glory of the new Israel, *76* having itself also been born by the Spirit, and seeing God face to face. And if the infant has been baptized, he brings it in even into the altar, and carries it round about, as though making an obeisance too, showing that it is an offering to God; and it does obeisance to him who made it. But if the child has not yet been baptized, standing before the rails, and making an obeisance at the altar through the infant, and handing it over to the mother, he dismisses them. From this point, then, the infant is a catechumen; and the mother has received leave both to enter the temple, as having been cleansed, and to partake of the mysteries, whenever she is ready and wishes. And these things are so.

CHAPTER 61. A contemplation of the things celebrated in the divine baptism.

Learn now the things of the most sacred ερωτάτου baptism; and first, that there is no set time of days or hours for it, lest at any time the infant should depart unenlightened. But if there be fear of death, all the things done according to the form are done at once, upon its being born; but with attention, and by a priest both reverent and sober [the Latin gives nisi quod abest oratio atque pius sanctusque sacerdos, except that the prayer and a pious and holy priest are lacking]; and if indeed the time allows, and there is no fear of death, at once after the sacred service, that the infant may also partake of the mysteries. And concerning this Dionysius says that all the mysteries perfect us by the communion. For this is the end of all. And the priest ought indeed both to study the sacred prayers well and to say them, and with attention, and slowly, unless there be some necessity, reading them in the hearing. For we have heard from the Fathers that those who are often terrified by phantasms suffer this for this reason, because the priests who baptized them did not say the exorcisms with attention, and the other sacred prayers. If, then, there is leisure, one ought to say the exorcisms from the beginning, and the other prayers, not once only, but many times. For indeed in the Euchologion it is written that they be said eight or ten times, because there was also an ancient custom of the Church that these were said to those being baptized on each day, for seven days, by the catechizing priests, and on the eighth again by the bishop, or by the priest who was about to baptize; and so they were baptized by the bishop or the priest. But we have seen reverent priests saying these three times without omission, and so baptizing. And the most divine baptism is done thus.

Τὸ βάπτισμαδὲ τὸ θειότατον γίνεται οὖτω· προσάεται ὰ έλλων ἃαπτισθῆναι, εἰ μὲν βρέφος, ὑπὸ γυναικ ς κατεχόμενον, καὶ τῶο ἀνοδόχου πρόντος· εἰ δ´ ἡλιΟΑΡΟΤ ουκοοΟ 213κίαν ἔχει, μόνος, ἐπεὶ καὶ ἑκουσίως δεῖ τοῦτον προσέρχεσθαι, καὶ ὁ ἀνάδοχος σὺν αὐτῷ. Αὐτὸς γὰρ αὐτοῦ ἐγγυητὴς εἶς Χριστὸν, ὥστε τηρεῖν τὰ τῆς πίστεως, καὶ χριστιανικῶς ζῇν, καὶ τὸ ὄνομα δὲ δηλοῖ. Ἀνάδοχος γὰρ, εἰς ἑαυτὸν οἷον ἀναδεχόενος. ΚΕΦΑΛ. ΞΒʹ. Οτι χρὴ τὸν ἀνάδοχον τοῦ βαπτιζομένου ὀρθόδοξοκ εἶναί καὶ εὐσεβῆ. Ἔνθα δὴ καὶ προσεκτέον, ἀναδόχους ποιεῖσθαι φιλευσεβεῖς καὶ διδασκάλους σχεδὸν τῆς πίστεως. Ἀλλ´ ἐμοὶ ἥκουσται λίαν ἄτοπον καὶ βαρύ. Τοὺς γὰρ ἠῴκτας καὶ ὑβριστὰς τῆς πίστεως, ἀθέους τε καὶ αέρετικοὺς, εἰς ἀναδοχὴνοι] τῶν τέκνων αὐτῶν τινες οἰα τι προσκαλουνται ἀνθρώπινον καὶ τὸ μυστήριον ἀθετοῦσιν οὐτοι· καὶ τὰ τέκνα οὐ φωτίζουσιν, ἀλλὰ σκοτίζουσι μᾶλλον. Καὶ ὁ ὑπουργῶν ἱερεὺς κοινωνὸς δείκνυται τοῖς ἀλλοτρίοις Θεοῦ. Καὶ πῶς ἅν ὁ βλασφημῶν εἰς Θεὸν τὴν εὐσέβειαν ἐκδιδάξοι; Ἀλλὰ τίς κοινωνία φωτὶ πρὸς σκότος Ἡ τίς μερὶς πΙστῷ μετὰ ἀπίστου, ὡς γέγεαπται· Ὁ τὰ τοιαῦτα ποιῶν κατάκριτος· καὶ ὁ τὰ των ἀστθῶν δεχόμενος βρέφη, εἰ μὴ πρότεοον ἅαθομολογήσωσιν οὖτοι ἅγιον εἶναι καὶ θεῖον τὸ βάπτισμα, καὶ ἄμαρτίας καθαρτικὸν, ἢ καὶ ἀναγεννων ἐν Δριστῷ, καὶ Χριστιανὸν εἶναι τὸν τοῦτο δεχόμενον Κγὰρ τοῦτο, ὁ μὲν ὅτι πιστὸν ἔσται τὸ βρέφος όμολογήσει, αὐτὸς δὲ ὑπὸ φόβου τινὸς ὑποκρύπτεταιὶ, καλὸν δέχεσθαι καὶ ἀγιάζειν τὸ βρέφς, μήποπε καὶ ληφθείη οὕτω παρὰ Θεοῦ. Ἵσταται οὐν σὼν τῶ ἀναδόχῳ ὁ βαπτιζόμενος, δεικνὺς, ὅτι ἐκολ σίως προσῆλθε, καὶ τὸν ἐγγυώμενον ἔχει. Καὶ τοῦ ἰερέως εὐλογήσαντος, εὐχαριστεῖν δεῖ ἐξ ἀρχῆς καὶ πάντοτε τῷ Θεῷ ἐπὶ τῇ τοῦ ἀπολωλότος σωτηρία, εὐθὴς γυμνῷ ὄντι καὶ ἀπερικαλύπτῳ μετὰ περιβολαίου μόνου, ἐμφυσᾷ τῷ κατηχουμένῳ σταυροειδῶς τρίτον ὁ ἱερεύς. Καὶ διὰ μὲν τοῦ ἐμφυσήματος τὴν ἐν τῷ Ἀδὰμ πρώτην ἐκείνην ἔμπνευσιν ἀναζωπυρεῖ, ἐπαὶ, «καὶ ενεφύσησεν εἰς τὸ πρόσωπον αὐτοῦ, φησὶ, πνοὴν ζωῆς.» Καὶ ὁ ἱερεὺς δὲ Χριστόν τὸν Θεὸν τυποῖ. Ὅθεν καὶ καλὸν μετὰ τὴν κοινωνίαν Υοισταῦ ὄαπτίζειν τε καὶ ἐμπνέειν. Τρίτον δὲ ἐμφυσᾷ διὰ τὴν Τριάδα, ὅτι κοινὴ τῆς Τριάδος ἐστὶν ἡ χάρις καὶ ἡ ἐνέργεια. Σταυροειδῶς δὲ διὰ τὸν σαρκωθέντα ὑπὲρ ἡμῶν, καὶ παθόντα σαρκὶ τῆς Τριάδος ἕνα Χριστὸν, καὶ τῷ σταυρῷ τὴν νίκην στησάμενον. Διὸ καὶ ὁ σταυρικὸς τύπος τὴν θυσίαν αὐτοῦ δηλοῖ, ταπείνωσίν τε καὶ νίκην, ὅτι δι´ αὐτοῦ τὰς ὀδύνας ἔλυσε τοῦ θανάτου, καὶ κατήργησε τὸν τὸ κοάτος ἔχοντα τοῦ θανάτου διάβολον, καὶ τὰς ἀντικειμένας ἐθριάμβευσε πονηροτάτας δυνάμεις. Ἐν ᾦ δὴ αταυρῷ καὶ ἡμεῖς νικῶμεν ἐν Χριστῷ τὸν διάβολον, οαὶ τοὺς δαίμονας ἐκτρεπόμεθα. Καὶ σημεῖον ὁ σταυρὸς τοῦ Σωτῆρος καὶ σφραγίς τε καὶ τρόπαιον.

He who is about to be baptized is brought, if an infant, held by a woman, with the sponsor present; but if he is of age, 213alone, since he must also come of his own will, and the sponsor with him. For he is his surety toward Christ, that he will keep the things of the faith, and live as a Christian; and the name too shows this. For ἀνάδοχος, sponsor, is as it were one who takes him up into himself.

CHAPTER 62. That the sponsor of him who is being baptized must be orthodox and pious.

Here indeed one must also take heed to make sponsors of lovers of piety, and almost teachers of the faith. But I have heard of something exceedingly out of place and grievous. For some call persecutors and insulters of the faith, godless men and heretics, to the sponsoring (alas!) of their children, for some human reason; and these set the mystery at nought; and they do not enlighten the children, but rather darken them. And the priest who ministers is shown to be a partaker with those who are strangers to God. And how would the blasphemer against God teach piety? But what communion has light with darkness? Or what part has a believer with an unbeliever, as it is written? He who does such things is condemned; and so is he who receives the infants of the impious, unless these first confess that baptism is holy and divine, and cleansing of sin, *77* and regenerating in Christ, and that he who receives it is a Christian (for if this is so, the one will confess that the infant will be faithful, while he himself hides under some fear), it is good to receive and sanctify the infant, lest it should also be taken thus by God. The one being baptized, then, stands with the sponsor, showing that he has come of his own will, and has the one who stands surety. And when the priest has blessed, for one must give thanks to God from the beginning and always over the salvation of him who was lost, at once, while he is naked and uncovered, with a wrapping only, the priest breathes upon the catechumen three times in the shape of a cross. And through the breathing-upon he rekindles that first inbreathing in Adam, since, «And he breathed into his face, it says, the breath of life.» And the priest represents Christ God. Whence it is also good to baptize and to inbreathe after the communion of Christ. And he breathes upon him three times on account of the Trinity, because the grace and the operation are common to the Trinity; and in the shape of a cross on account of him who was incarnate for us, and suffered in the flesh, Christ, one of the Trinity, and set up the victory by the cross. Wherefore also the cruciform figure signifies his sacrifice, both humiliation and victory, because through it he loosed the pangs of death, and abolished him who had the power of death, the devil, and triumphed over the most wicked opposing powers. By which cross we too conquer the devil in Christ, and turn the demons away. And the cross is a sign of the Saviour, and a seal, and a trophy.

Τρὶς οὖν ἐμφυσήσας τῷ κατηχουμένῳ ὁ ἱερεὺς, καὶ τρὶς σφραγίσας πάλιν τῇ χειρὶ τῷ σημείῳ τούτω τῷ σταυρικῷ διὰ τὸ, «Αἰ χεῖρές σου ἐποίησάν με καὶ ἔπλασάν με,» καὶ ἐν τῷ μετώπῳ τε, ὡς ποοειρήκειν, διὰ τὸ νοητικὸν, καὶ ἐν τῷ στόματι διὰ τὸ λογικὸν, καὶ ἐν τῇ καρδίᾳ διὰ τὸ ζωτικὸν, ἐπιτίΔΛΡΕΤ 216θησι τὴν χεῖρα αὐτῷ ἡγιασμένην οὖσαν, καῖ ἐν τῷ Πνεύματι πλάττουσαν· καὶ τὰς ἱερὰς εὐχὸς ἐπιλέγει, Ἀλλὰ πρὸ τοῦ ἐμπνεῦσαι καὶ σφραγίσαι, ὡς εἴρηται, ἀπολύει τῆς ζώνης καὶ ἀποδύει τὸν κατηχούμενον, ἀσκεπῆ τε τὴν κεφαλήν, καὶ τοὺς πόδας γυμνίν παρίστησι, κατὰ ἀνατολὰς ὁρῶντα. Τί ἐν τούτῳ δηλῶν; Ὅτι ὁ προσερχόμενος τῷ Θεῷ, καὶ πρὸς τὸ φῶς τὸ ἀληθινὸν ἥδη βλέπων, ὃ δηλοῖ ἡ ἀνατολὴ κεῖθεν γὰρ ἔρχεται τὸ φῶς), ἐλεύθερος χρὴ εἶναι σῶν παθῶν, καὶ λελυμένος τῆς ἀπιστίας, καὶ γυμνὸς πάσης κακίας, καὶ περιβλήματος ἁμαρτίας ἀλλότριος, καὶ τὴν δουλείαν ὑποδεικνὺς τῷ Θεῷ.

Διὰ τοῦτο τοίνυν ἵσταται γυμνός. Καὶ ἔτι δὲ, ὃιὰ τὸ γυμνὸν γεγονέναι τῇ ἁμαρτία τὸν ἄνθρωπον, καὶ τὴν θεοθφαντον ἀπολέσαι περιβολὴν, τοῦτο τὸ σχῆμα τῆς γυμνώσεως μαρτυρεῖ. Ἐπειδὴ γὰρ προσέρχεται, τὸν ῥύπον τῆς ἁμαρτίας καὶ τῆς ἀπιστίας ἀποβαλέσθαι, καὶ ὅλος γενέσθαι νέος, καὶ τὴν μορφὴν τοῦ νέου Ἀδὰμ ἐνδύσασθαι καὶ εἰς τὸ κρεῖττον ἢ πρότερον ὑπῆρχεν ἐπανελθεῖν, τὴν γύμνωσιν δείκνυσι, καὶ μαρτυρεῖ τὴν αἰσχύνην· καὶ οὔτως ἱστάμενος ἐκκαθαίρεται, τὴν ἀναμόρφωσιν ἐκζητῶν. Ἵα δὲ μὴ μηκύνοντες τὸν λόγον κόρον ταῖς ἀκοαῖς ἐμποιήσωμεν, κεφαλαιωῶς εἴπωμεν ἤδη περὶ τοῦ προκειμένου. Ὁ μὲν ἀρχιερεὺς καὶ ἱερεὺς βαπτίζων, αὐτὸν ἐκτυποῖ τὸν Κύριον, τὸν ἐν πᾶσι τὰ πάντα ποιοῦντα, ἐπεὶ καὶ τὴν αὐτοῦ φέρει δύναμιν, τὸν καὶ πλάσαντα πρότερον, ταὶ αὖθις ἀναπλάττοντα· ὁ βαπτιζόμενος δὲ τὸν !παραπεσόντα Ἀδὰμ, ἐπεὶ καὶ τὸ προσέρχεσθαι, τὸ ἐκούσιον τῆς γνώμης δηλοῖ. Εἰ δὲ νήπιος παρά τινος πιστοῦ προσαγόμενος, τὸ προσάγεσθαι ἐν πίστει καὶ μὴ κατ´ ἀνάγκην σημαίνει, ὁ ἀναδεχόμενος δὲ τὸν ἐγγυώμενον κατά τε πίστιν καὶ βίον. Τὸ ἀποδύεσθαι δὲ καὶ ἀπολύεσθαι ἑστῶτα γυμνὸν μετὰ περιζώματος τὸ ῥίπτειν πᾶσαν κακίαν, καὶ των δεσμῶν τῆς ἁμαρτίας ἐλευθεροῦσθαι, καὶ γυμνὸν τῶν τῆς πλάνης τε καὶ φθορᾶς προσέρχεσθαι τῷ Χριστῶ· καὶ ὅτι γυμνὸς γέγονε πλανηθείς, τὴν πρὶν ἦ ἀπολέσας στολὴν, ἡν νῦν ἔρχεται λαμπρότερον διὰ τοῦ βαπτίσματος ἐπενδύσασθαι. Πρὸς ἀατολὰς δὲ ὁρᾷ, τὸ φῶς τὸ ἀληθινὸν ἐκζητῶν, ὅπερ ἐστὶν ἡ Τριὰς, καὶ ὁ εἶἷς τῆς Τριάδος Χριατὸς σαρκωθεὶς, ἐλθὼν ἐκ Θαιμάν, ἤτοι ἐξ ἀνατολῶν, ἀλλὰ καὶ τὸν παράδεισον οὖ ἐκπέπτωκε φυτευθέντα κατὰ ἀνατολάς. Ἐμφυσᾶται δὲ παρὰ τοῦ ἁρχιερέως, διὰ τὸ πρῶτον ἐμφύσημα, ἀνακαινίζεται γάρ. Τρίτον δὲ ἐμφυσᾶται, διὰ τὴν θείαν ἐνέργειαν τῆς ζωοποιοῦ Τριάδος. Δι αὐτῆς γὰρ ἐπλάσθη, καὶ ἀναπλάττεται. Σφραγίζεται δὲ χειρὶ ἀρχιερέως διὰ σταυροῦ, ὅτι χεῖρες Θεοῦ ἡμᾶς ἔπλασαν, ὁ Υἱὸς καὶ τὸ Πνεῦμα. Ὁ σταυρὸς δὲ σημεῖον Χριστοῦ καὶ τρόπαιον. Δι αὐτοῦ γὰρ νικήσας, τοὺς δαίμονας ἔθραυσε· καὶ νῦν ἀπ´ αὐτοῦ, ἐκτυπουμένου τοῦ σταυροῦ, τὰ δαιμόνια φεύγει. Ἐν τῷ μετώπῳ δὲ πρῶτον σφραγίζεται, ἵν´ ἀγιασθῇ ὁ τελούμενος τὸν νοῦν, τῆς πλάνης φυγαδευθείσης, Δεύτερον δὲ τὸ στόμα, ἵνα τὸν λόγον ἁγιασθῇ. καὶ ἀνομολογῆ τὴν ἀλήθειαν.

Having, then, breathed upon the catechumen three times, the priest, having also sealed him three times again with his hand with this cruciform sign, on account of the «Thy hands have made me and fashioned me,» both on the forehead, as I said before, for the intellectual part, and on the mouth for the rational part, and on the heart for the vital part, lays 216his hand upon him, which is sanctified and fashions in the Spirit; and he pronounces over him the sacred prayers. But before breathing upon him and sealing, as has been said, he frees the catechumen of his girdle and unclothes him, and presents him bareheaded, and with his feet bare, looking toward the east. What does he show in this? That he who approaches God, and now looks toward the true light, which the east signifies (for thence comes the light), must be free of the passions, and loosed from unbelief, and stripped of all wickedness, and a stranger to the garment of sin, and showing forth his servitude to God.

For this reason, then, he stands naked. And further, because man became naked through sin, and lost the God-woven garment, this figure of nakedness bears witness to it. For since he comes to cast off the filth of sin and of unbelief, and to become wholly new, and to put on the form of the new Adam, and to return to what is better than what was before, he shows the nakedness, and bears witness to the shame; and standing thus he is cleansed, seeking the re-formation. But that we may not, by lengthening the discourse, produce satiety in the ears, let us now speak summarily about the matter in hand. The bishop and priest who baptizes figures the Lord himself, who works all things in all, since he also bears his power — him who also fashioned us before, and refashions us again; and the one being baptized figures the Adam who had fallen away, since his approaching too shows the willingness of his purpose. And if it is an infant brought by some faithful person, the being brought signifies in faith and not by compulsion; and he who undertakes for him signifies the one who stands surety, both as to faith and as to life. The putting off of clothes and the being loosed, standing naked with a loincloth, signify the casting away of all wickedness, and being freed from the bonds of sin, and coming to Christ naked of the things of error and corruption; and that he became naked when he went astray, having lost the former *78* robe, which he now comes to put on more splendidly through baptism. And he looks toward the east, seeking the true light, which is the Trinity, and the one of the Trinity, Christ, incarnate, who came from Thaiman, that is, from the east; and also the paradise, from which he fell, planted toward the east. And he is breathed upon by the bishop on account of the first breathing-upon; for he is being renewed. And he is breathed upon three times, on account of the divine operation of the life-giving Trinity. For by it he was fashioned, and is refashioned. And he is sealed by the bishop's hand by means of the cross, because the hands of God fashioned us, the Son and the Spirit. And the cross is a sign of Christ and a trophy. For having conquered through it, he shattered the demons; and now, when the cross is traced, the demons flee from it. And he is sealed first on the forehead, that the one being initiated may be sanctified in mind, error having been put to flight. And second the mouth, that he may be sanctified in his word, and confess the truth.

Καὶ τρίτον τὸ στὴθος, ἵνα τὴν κορδίαν ᾖ καθαρὸς, καὶ Θεὸν δρᾷ, 217καὶ μηδὲν κρύττη, καὶ ἐν μὐτῷ ζῇ, καὶ κινῆται καὶ Κμένῃ. Μετὰ τοῦτο εὐλογεῖ τὸν Θεὸν ὁ ἀρχιερεὺς, ὅτι ἄξιον αὐτὸν εὐλογεῖν ἐπὶ τῇ τοῦ ἀπολωλότος εὑρέσει, καὶ τῇ ἀνθρωπίνῃ σωτηρίᾳ. Εἰτα χειρὸς ἐπίθεσις διὰ τὴν μεταδιδομένην χάριν καὶ τὴν ἀνάπλασιν. Ἡ πρώτη δὲ εὐχὴ ἐν ὁνόματι τῆς Τριάδος, ὐτῇ γὰρ παρατίθεται τῷ μόνῳ Θεῷ, καὶ δι´ αὐτῆς Κἀγιάζεται. Καὶ ἀνεξάρνητον μένειν τὸ ὄνομα τῆς Τριάδος ἐν τῷ βαπτιζομένῳ ἡ εὐχὴ δυσωπεῖ, καὶ σελειιωθῆναι τοῦτον τῶ θείῳ βαπτίσματί τε καὶ Χρίσματι, καὶ τῇ ἱερᾷ Κοινωνία. Ἡ δέ γε δευτέρα εὐχὴ ἀφορκισμός ἐστιν, ἀποσοβοῦσα καὶ διώκουσα τὸν διάβολον, καὶ πᾶσαν ἐνέργειαν αὐτοῦ τοῖς φρικτοῖς καὶ θείοις ὀνόμασί τε καὶ μυστηρίοις. Καὶ τοῦ ἀνθρώπου διώκει τοῦτον, καὶ τοὺς αὐτοῦ δαίμονας. Καὶ φεύγειν ἐκ τούτου παραγγέλλει, καὶ μὴ ἐπηρεάζειν αὐτῷ. Ὁμοίως δὲ καὶ ἡ τρίτῃ ἀφορκισμὸς, οὐσα καὶ αὐτὴ, τοὺς δαίμονας θείῳ ὀνόματι ἐκδιώκαι. Ἢ δέ γε τετάρτη πάλιν εὐχὴ πρὸς τὸν Θεόν εστιν ἀναφερομένη, τὸν πονηρὸν παντελῶς ἐκδιῶξαι τοῦ πλάσματος τοῦ θείου ἐξαιτουμένη, καὶ ἐν τὴ πίστει στηρίξαι. Ὅθεν εὐθὺς καὶ τὴν πέμπτην ποιεῖται εὐχὴν, ἀναγκαιοτέραν οὖσαν, καὶ δι´ αὐτῆς ἀνατίθησι τὸν βαπτιζόμενον τῷ Θεῷ. Ἐν ᾖ καὶ πάλιν εὐχῇ ἐμφυσᾷ αὐτῷ τὸ δεύτερον· ὅπερ ἐστὶ τὸν βεβαιότερον ἔργον, τῆς ἀναζωώσεώς τε καὶ ἀνακαινίσεως ἑκ τούτου δηλουμένου. Καὶ σφραγίζει τρὶς αὖθις κατά τε τὸ μέτωπον, καὶ τὸ στόμα, καὶ στῆθος, διὰ τὸν νοῦν τε καὶ λόγον αὐτοῦ καὶ τὴν ζωήν· καὶ ὅτι νοῦς καὶ λόγος καὶ Πνεῦμα ἡ Τριὰς ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν, ἡ τοῦτον ἀναζωοῦσα, καὶ τὸ κατ´ εἰκόνα Ἀιδοῦσα, ὅπερ ἀπώλεσε. «Καὶ ἐξέλασον ἀπ´ αὐτοῦ, φησὶ, πᾶν πονηρὸν καὶ ἀκάθαρτον πνεῦμα, κεκρυμμένον καὶ ἐμφωλεῦον αὐτοῦ τῇ καρδίᾳ.» Ἐμφιλοχωοοῦσι γὰρ τὰ δαιμόνια ταῖς τῶν ἀβαπτίστων ψυναῖς, ἀποπλανᾷν καὶ μιαίνειν βουλόμενα. Καὶ τοῦτο ἱσχύουσιν ἀπὸ τῆς παραβάσεως ἐκείνης. Διὸ ταῖς καρδίαις τῶν ἁμυήτων τε καὶ ἀπίστων ἐνοικοῦσι προδήλως, καὶ ταύτας μιαίνουσί τε καὶ πρὸς τὰ εομισῆ συγκινοῦσιν. Ἐν τῷ τέλει δὲ τῆς εὐχῆς ἡ ὰποταγὴ καὶ συνταγὴ οὕτω γίνεται. Στρέφει τὸν βαπτιζόμενον ὁ ἀρχιερεὺς ὡς πρὸς δυσμάς, τοῦ σκότους ἐκ τούτου δηλουμένου, ἐν ᾧ ἐστιν ὁ διάβολος. Αἶρειν τε τὰς χεῖρας αὐτὸν ἄνω κελεύει, δεικνυομένου, ὅτι οὐκ ἔχει τι ὁ βαπτιζόμενος κεκρυμμένον ἐν ἑαυτῷ τῶν τοῦ πονηροῦ· καὶ ὅτι τὸν ἐσταυρωμένον ὑπὲρ ἡμῶν ἄρχεται ἐκμιμεῖσθαι. Καὶ λέτειν παρακελεύεται παρὰ τοῦ ἁρχιερέως, «Ἀποτάσσομαι τῷ Σατανᾷ, καὶ πᾶσι τοῖς ἔργοις αὐτοῦ,» καὶ τὰ ἐφεξῆς. Καὶ ὁ βαπτιζόμενος τὴν ἀποταγὴν τοῦ πονηροῦ ποιεῖται, καὶ τῶν αὐτοῦ τὴν τελείαν ἐξάρνησιν. Καὶ τοῦτό φησι τρὶς, εἰς βεβαίωσιν τῆς ἀποταγῆς τοῦ ἐχθροῦ· καὶ ὅτι φεύγει ὁλοήύχως τὸν πονηρόν· ἕαι δὲ καὶ εἰς τιμὴν τῆς ἀγίας Τριάδος, ἥτις πάντα βεξαιοῖ τε καὶ τελειοῖ.

And third the breast, that he may be pure in heart, and see God, 217and hide nothing, and live in him, and move, and abide. After this the bishop blesses God, because it is fitting to bless him for the finding of what was lost, and for the salvation of man. Then the laying on of a hand, on account of the grace that is imparted and the refashioning. And the first prayer is in the name of the Trinity. For by it he is committed to the only God, and through it he is sanctified. And the prayer entreats that the name of the Trinity remain undenied in the one being baptized, and that he be perfected by the divine baptism and chrism, and by the sacred communion. And the second prayer is an exorcism, scaring off and pursuing the devil and all his operation by the dread and divine names and mysteries. And it drives him out of the man, and his demons. And it charges him to flee from him, and not to molest him. Likewise the third too, being itself also an exorcism, drives out the demons by the divine name. And the fourth prayer, again, is one offered up to God, asking that he wholly drive out the evil one from the divine handiwork, and establish him in the faith. Whence he at once makes the fifth prayer also, which is more necessary, and through it he dedicates the one being baptized to God. In which prayer too he again breathes upon him a second time; which is the firmer work, the re-quickening and the renewal being shown by this. And he seals him three times again on the forehead, and the mouth, and breast, on account of his mind and word and life; and because the Trinity, our God, is mind and word and Spirit, which re-quickens him and gives that which is according to the image, which he lost. «And drive out from him, it says, every evil and unclean spirit, hidden and lurking in his heart.» For the demons love to dwell in the souls of the unbaptized, wishing to lead them astray and defile them. And they have this power from that transgression. Wherefore they dwell openly in the hearts of the uninitiated and unbelieving, and both defile them and stir them up to the things hateful to God. And at the end of the prayer the renunciation and the joining take place thus. The bishop turns the one being baptized as toward the west, the darkness being shown by this, in which the devil is. And he bids him raise his hands up, it being shown that the one being baptized has nothing of the things of the evil one hidden within himself; and that he begins to imitate him who was crucified for us. And he is bidden by the bishop to say, «I renounce Satan, and all his works,» and what follows. And the one being baptized makes the renunciation of the evil one, and the perfect denial of his things. And this he says three times, for the confirmation of the renunciation of the enemy; and because he flees from the evil one with his whole soul; and further also for the honour of the holy Trinity, which confirms *79* and perfects all things.

Ἐρωτήσας δὲ πάλιν τρὶς, αἈπετάξοω τῶ Σατανᾶς» καὶ τοῦ βαπτιζομένου συν. θεμένου τρὶς, καὶ εἰπόντος, «Ἀπεταξάμην,» τουτέστιν ἠρνησάμην τε καὶ ἀπεβαλόμην τὸν πονηρὸν, φα 220ἵήν, ὁ ἀρχιερεὺς, «Ἐμφύσησον αὐτῷ,» φηοί, τουτέστιν ἐκ τῆς καρδίας ἀπόῤῥιψον, καὶ ἀπὸ καοδίας ἀθέτησον, καὶ καταίσχυνον τὸν ἐχθρόν. Καὶ τοῦτο ποιεῖται τρὶς διὰ τὸ ἀσφαλὲές τῆς ἀποταγῆς τοῦ πονηροῦ, καὶ εἰς δόξαν τοῦ ἐν Τριάδι μόνου Θεοῦ ἡμῶν, τοῦ καταβαλόντος τὸν πονηρόν. χΕἶτα στρέφει τοῦτον κατὰ ἀνατολάς· πρὸς τὸ φῶς γὰρ ἀνάγει ἀπὸ τοῦ σκότους, καὶ ἐκ τοῦ ψεύδους πρὸς τὴν ἀλήθειαν. Καί φησιν ὁ ἀρχιερεύς· «Εἰπὲ, Καὶ συντάσσομαι τῶ Χριστῷ, καὶ πιστεύω εἰς ἕνα Θεὸν Πατέρα παντοκράτορα, » καὶ τὰ ἑξῆς. Δεῖ γὰρ αὐτὸν τὸ τρισήλιον καθαρῶς ἀνομολογῆσαι φῶς, ὡς ἂν καὶ τῇ καρδία δέξηται τὴν λάμψιν αὐτοῦ. Τὴν ὁμολογίαν οὖν τοῦ ἱεροῦ Συμβόλου πᾶσαν λαμπρῶς τῷ στόματι λέγει Καὶ τοῦτο ποιεῖται τρὶς, βεβαιῶν τὴν ὁμολογίαν, ἅμα δὲ καὶ εἰς τιμὴν, ὡς εἰρήκαυεν τῆς τρισυποστάτου Θεότητος. Ὁμολογεῖ δὲ τοῦτο φανερῶς, ἐπεὶ τὴν ἀλήθειαν καταγγέλλει· καὶ ὅτι ἐνώπιον ἀγγέλων δεῖ καὶ ἀνθρώπων ταύτην κηρύσσειν, δι´ ἦς καὶ σω. θήσεται. Καὶ τὸν Θεὸν τῶν ὅλων καταγγέλλειν χρὴ παῤῥησίᾳ, τὴν ὁμοούσιόν φημι Τριάδα, τὸν μόνον ὄντως ὄντα Θεὸν, ἀλλὰ καὶ τοῦ ἑνὸς τῆς Τριάδος τοῦ Θεοῦ Λόγου τὴν ὑπὲρ ἡμῶν ἐκ Παρθένου σάρκωσιν, Τοῦτο οὖν ποιησαμένου τρὶς, καὶ ἐρωτηθέντος αὐθις τρὶς, «Συνετάξω τῷ Χριστῷ;» καὶ ἀποκριθέντος τρὶς ἐκ καρδίας, καὶ εἰρηκότος, «Συνεταξάμην αὐτῷ, » ὁ ἀρχιερεύς φησι, «Καὶ προσκύνησον αὐτῷ,» καὶ σφραγισαμένου σταυρῷ διὰ τῆς χειρὸς αὐτοῦ, ὃ τὴν τοῦ Χριστοῦ ὁμολογίαν δηλοῖ καὶ σημείωσιν, καὶ προσκυνήσαντος τρὶς, ἐνδεικνυμένου τὴν δουλείαν, καὶ τὴν ἀληθῆ μετὰ τῆς ὁμολογίας ὑποταγὴν, στρέφεται πρὸς ἀνατολὰς, καὶ εὐλογεῖ τὸν Θεὸν ἄἀρχιερεὺς τὸν πάντας σωθῆναι θέλοντα, Οὕτω γὰρ δεῖ εὐχαριστεῖν ἐπὶ πᾶσι, καὶ μᾶλλον τῇ τῶν ἀνθρώπων σωτηρίᾳ.

Καὶ οὕτω κρατήσας αὐτὸν τὸν κατηχούμενον τῆς χειρὸς ὡς τὸν ἀπολωλότα εὑρὼν, εἰσάγει εἰς τὸν ναὸν, ὡς εἰς τὸν οὐρανὸν δηλονότι, ἢ τὸν παράδεισον, δεικνὸς, ὡς προσφέρει τοῦτον τῷ ἀπολωλεκότι δεσπότῃ. Τὴν χαρὰν δὲ δηλῶν, ἣν μετὰ τῶν ἀγγέλων χαίρει ἐπὶ μετανοοῦντι ἀμαρτωλῷ, «Χαῖρε, κεχαριτωμένη,» φησὶν ὑμνῶν, καὶ εἰσέρχεται. Αὕτη γὰρ ἡ ἀπαρχὴ τῆς σωτηρίου χαρᾶς, ἡ σωτήριος πύλη, ἡ τὴν σωτηρίαν ἡμῖν οἰκονομήσασα, καὶ τὴν ἀληθῆ χαρὰν τῷ κόσμῳ, τὸν Θεὸν Λόγον σεσαρκωμένον ἐξ αὐτῆς γεννήσασα ἀγίως τε καὶ παρθενικῶς εἰς ἡμετέραν ἀνάπλασιν. Καὶ τὰ τοῦ Συμεὼν δὲ ἐκτυπῶν ὁ ἀρχιερεὺς, προσάγει τοῦ θυσιαστηρίου ἐνώπιον τὸν βαπειζόμενον, δηλῶν, ὡς ὅτι προσέφερε. Καὶ ποιεῖ τοῦτον τρὶς προσκυνῆσαι Θεῷ· ὅτι καὶ κατήλλακται τῷ Θεῷ δι´ επιστροφῆς, ὥσπερ ἐκεῖνος ὁ ἄσωτος, καὶ πρὸς τὴν υἱοθεσίαν καὶ ἀφθαρσίαν τὴν πρώτην στολὴν τοῦ Πνεύματος, ὅσον οὔπω ἔρχεται μειζόνως τε καὶ κρειττόνως, διὰ τοῦ θείου βαπτίσματος. Ἵνα δὲ καὶ ἠ ἐπιστροφὴ καὶ ἡ ὁμολογίαν ἀσφαλὴς ᾖ, καὶ τὸν ἀνάδοχον καλεῖ ὁ ἀρχιερεύς. Καὶ προσκυνῆσαι τρὶς λέγει τῷ θυσιαστηρίῳ, ἐν ψπερ οἰκεῖ ὁ Θεὸς, διὰ τούτου δηλῶν, ὡς λόγον παρέχει, φυλάξαι ἀσφαλῶς ἐν τῇ πίστει καὶ τῇ εὐσεβεῖ πολιτείᾳ τὸν βαπτιζόμενον.

And having asked again three times, «Hast thou renounced Satan?» and the one being baptized having agreed three times, and said, «I have renounced,» [the Latin gives the reply in the plural, Abrenuntiamus … Abnegamus et rejicimus, we have renounced … we deny and cast off] that is, I have denied and cast off the evil one — for the renunciation of evil must be manifest and firm — 220the bishop says, «Breathe upon him,» that is, cast him out of thy heart, and set him at nought from the heart, and put the enemy to shame. And this is done three times, for the security of the renunciation of the evil one, and to the glory of our God, the only one in Trinity, who cast down the evil one. Then he turns him toward the east; for he leads him up from the darkness to the light, and from the lie to the truth. And the bishop says, «Say, And I join myself to Christ, and I believe in one God, the Father almighty,» and what follows. For he must confess purely the thrice-sunned light, so that he may receive its brightness in the heart too. He says, then, the whole confession of the sacred Symbol splendidly with the mouth. And this is done three times, confirming the confession, and at the same time also for the honour, as we have said, of the three-hypostatic Godhead. And he confesses this openly, since he proclaims the truth; and because he must proclaim it before angels and men, through which he will also be saved. And one must proclaim with boldness the God of all things — the consubstantial Trinity, I mean, the only truly existing God — and also the incarnation from the Virgin, for our sake, of the one of the Trinity, the Word of God. When, then, he has done this three times, and has been asked again three times, «Hast thou joined thyself to Christ?» and has answered three times from the heart, and said, «I have joined myself to him,» [the Latin gives the reply in the plural, Fatemur eum, we confess him] the bishop says, «And do obeisance to him,» and when he has sealed himself with the cross by his hand, which signifies the confession of Christ and a marking, and has done obeisance three times, showing his servitude, and his true subjection together with the confession, the bishop turns toward the east and blesses God, who wills all to be saved. For thus one must give thanks for all things, and most of all for the salvation of men.

And so, having taken the catechumen himself by the hand, as one who has found him that was lost, he brings him into the temple, as into heaven, plainly, or into paradise, showing that he is bringing him to the Master who had lost him. And showing the joy which he rejoices with the angels over a sinner who repents, he says, singing, «Hail, thou that art full of grace,» and goes in. For she is the first-fruits of the saving joy, the saving gate, she who dispensed salvation for us, and who bore for the world the true joy, God the Word made flesh from her, holily and virginally, for our refashioning. And the bishop, figuring forth the things of Symeon, brings the one being baptized before the altar, showing how that he offered him. And he makes him bow down three times to God; because he too has been reconciled to God through conversion, like that prodigal, and to the adoption and incorruption, the first robe of the Spirit, he is coming in next to no time, in a greater and better manner, through the divine baptism. And so that both the conversion and the confession may be secure, the bishop also calls the sponsor. And he tells him to bow down three times to the altar, in which God dwells, showing by this that he gives his word to guard the one being baptized securely in the faith and in pious conduct of life.

Ετα εἰ μὲν βρέφος 221ἐσεὶν ὁ μένλων βαπτίζεσθαι, κύψας λαμβάνει τοῦτον ὁ ἀνάδοχος, ἐνώπιον κείμενον τῶν ἁγίων θυρῶν, Κῶσπερ ἀπὸ τῶν χειρῶν τοῦ Χριστοῦ· εἰ δ´ ἐν ἡλικίᾳ, λαμβάνει τὸν βαπτιζόμενον ἀπὸ τῆς χειρὸς, τὸ αὐτὸ δηλῶν, ὅτι ἀπὸ τοῦ Χριστοῦ τοῦτον παρέλαβε, καὶ ἐγγυητὴς γέγονεν αὐτοῦ εἰς Χριστὸν, καὶ πνευματικὸς ἔσται πατήρ. Ὅθεν καὶ κρατῶν. προσοέρε· τοῶ. τον τῇ ἱερᾷ κολυμβήθρᾳ, δεικνὺς, ὡς ἑκουσίως καὶ οὐ κατὰ βίαν προσέρχεται τῷ βαπτίσματι ὁ τελούμενος, καὶ ἐπὶ μάρτυσι πρῶτον μὲν καὶ ἐξαιοέτως τῷ βΟ Θεῷ, ἔπειτα δὲ τῷ ἱεράρχῃ καὶ τοῖς παρατυχουσι πιστοῖς, οἶ πάντες μάρτυρες τῶν τολουμένων εἰσὶ, καὶ αὐτῷ δὴ τῷ ἀναδόχῳ τῷ καὶ προσάγοντι τοὺτον, καὶ μάλιστα δὴ τοῖς θείοις καὶ πανάγνοις ἀγγέλοις, ἀοράτως τότε παροῦσι, καὶ εὐφραινομένοις τῇ τοὺ βοπτιζομένου ἐν Πνεύματι θεογενεσίᾳ καὶ σωτηρίᾳ, καὶ ἀπογραφομένοις αὐτὸν ἐν τοῖς οὐρανοῖς, καὶ Κγνωρίμοις, καὶ φίλοις γινομένοις, ἀπό τε τῆς ἐκ τοῦ ίείου λουτροῦ καθάρσεως, καὶ τῆς σφραγῖδος τοῦ μύρου. Διὸ καὶ ἀναγκαῖον παρεῖναι τότε μετ´ εὐλαβείας τε καὶ χαρᾶς ἅπαντας σχεδὸν τοὺς πιστοὺς, καὶ πιστεύειν ὡς πάρεισιν ἄγγελοι, οἱ χαίρουσιν ἐπὶ ἑνὶ ἁμαρτωλῷ μετανοοῦντι· καὶ ὅτι παραδίδοται τότε ὁ βαπτιζόμενος ἁγίῳ ἀγγέλῳ φύλακι· καὶ οὖτος ἔσται πάντοτε μετ´ αὐτοῦ, τὰ φθονερὰ διώκων δαικόνια, καὶ πᾶσαν ἐπήρειαν τοῦ δολιωτάτου ἐχροῦ, εἴγε μόνον ὁ βαπτισθείς καθαρὸν τηρήσει πί ατει καὶ ἔογοις τὸ βάπτι σμα, καὶ εἰς τὴν βασιλείαν Ἐασώσει τοῦτον τῶν οὐρανῶν. Ὅτι οὖν μέγιστον τὸ ἕργον τοῦ θειοτάτου βαπτίσματος, διὰ τοῦτο πρότερον καὶ ἡ Ἑκκλησία διὰ τῶν ἀρχιερέων ἐν ταῖς καθολικαῖς ἐκκλησίαις ἐποιεῖτο τὰ τῶν προσερχομένων βαπτίσματα, καὶ καθ´ ὃν καιρὸν ἐν ταῖς ἑορταῖς τῆ θείᾳ λειτουργίᾳ, τὸ, «Ὅσοι εἰς Χριστὸν ἐβαπτίσθητε, » ψάλλομεν, καὶ μάλιστα δὴ τῷ μεγάλῳ Σιββάτω πρὸ τῆς θείας ἱερουργίας, τῶν πολλῶν ἀναγινωσκομένων ἀναγνωσμάτων ἀπὸ τῶν θείων προφι τῶν. Ἐπειδὴ τὸ ἱερώτατον βάπτισμα καὶ ἀναγέννησίς ἑστι, καὶ ἀνάπλασις, κάθαρσίς τε καὶ φωτισμὸς καὶ «Ιοθεσία, καὶ χάρισμα καὶ ἁγιασμὸς, καὶ μυρία ὅσα φασὶν οἱ Πατέρες. Τυποῖ δὲ ἐξαιρέτως τὸν θάνασον τοῦ Χριστοῦ, καὶ τὴν τριήμερον ἔγερσιν. Ὃ δὴ καὶ ἄ καιρὸς ἑκεῖνος καθαρώτερον παριστᾷ. Ἀπὸ γὰρ τοῦ θανάτου τοῦ Χριστοῦ καὶ τῆς τριημέρου ταφῆς καὶ τῆς ἀναστάσεως τὴν ἀθανασίαν καὶ ἀφθαρσίαν ἐλάβομεν, καὶ τὴν μέλλουσαν ἐκείνην ἐλπίζομεν ἀνάστασίν τε καὶ ἀνακαίνισιν, καὶ ζωήν τὴν αἰώνιον, ἧς γέγονε Χριστὸς ἀπαρχὴ, ὁ μόνος ὢν ἀναμάρτητος, παθὼν ὑπὲρ ἡμῶν τῶν ἁμαρτωλῶν, καὶ ὁ μὴ ὀφείλων ἀποθανεῖν, ὡς μὴ ἁμαρτήσας ὅλως, ἂπὲρ ἡμῶν ἁποθανών. Ἡ γὰρ ἁμαρτία τοῦ θανάτου ἐατὶ τὸ κέντρον, ὡς Ἡ σαϊας καὶ Παῦλος λέγουσιν, ἤτοι ἡ ἀρχὴ καὶ ῥίζα καὶ πρόξενος. Λύτὸς δὲ ὁ xριστὸς μὴ πεποιηκὼς ἁμαρτίαν, καὶ μὴ ὀφείλων ἀποθανεῖν, ἀπέθανε δι´ ἡμᾶς· καὶ διὰ τοῦτο καταργέσας τὸν θάνατον, ἐξεγήγερται, καὶ τὰ ἡμέτερα πάθη καὶ τὴν κατάκρισιν ἔλυσε, παθὼν ὁ ἀπαθής ἓι´ ἐαυτοῦ· γενόμενός τε ἀρχηγὸς τῆς ζωῆς ἡμῶν, εἰς τοὺς οὐρανοὺς ἀνελήφθη.

Then, if the one about to be baptized 221is an infant, the sponsor, stooping, takes him as he lies before the holy doors, as from the hands of Christ; but if he is of age, he takes the one being baptized by the hand, showing the same thing, that he has received him from Christ, and has become his surety unto Christ, and will be his spiritual father. Wherefore also, holding him, he brings him to the sacred font, showing that the one being initiated comes to baptism willingly and not by force, and before witnesses: first and pre-eminently *80* God, and then the hierarch and the faithful who happen to be present, who are all witnesses of what is being performed, and the sponsor himself, who also brings him forward, and above all the divine and all-pure angels, invisibly present then, and rejoicing at the birth from God in the Spirit and the salvation of the one being baptized, and enrolling him in the heavens, and becoming his acquaintances and friends, from the cleansing out of the divine laver and the seal of the myron. Wherefore it is also necessary that then nearly all the faithful be present with reverence and joy, and believe that angels are present, who rejoice over one sinner who repents; and that the one being baptized is then handed over to a holy guardian angel; and this one will be with him always, driving off the envious demons and every assault of the most guileful enemy, if only the one baptized keeps his baptism pure in faith and works; and he will bring him safe through into the kingdom of the heavens. Since, then, the work of the most divine baptism is very great, for this reason formerly the Church too, through the bishops, used to perform the baptisms of those who came forward in the catholic churches, and at the time when, on the feasts at the divine liturgy, we chant «As many as were baptized into Christ,» and above all on the Great Sabbath before the divine sacred service, while the many readings from the divine prophets are being read. Since the most sacred baptism is both regeneration and refashioning, cleansing and illumination and adoption, and gift and sanctification, and the countless things, as many as the Fathers say. And it typifies pre-eminently the death of Christ, and the rising on the third day. Which indeed that season too presents more purely. For from the death of Christ and the three days' burial and the resurrection we received immortality and incorruption, and we hope for that resurrection and renewal to come, and the life eternal, of which Christ has become the first-fruits, he who alone is sinless, having suffered for us sinners, and he who was not obliged to die, as not having sinned at all, having died for us. For sin is the sting of death, as Isaiah and Paul say, that is, its beginning and root and procurer. And Christ himself, not having done sin, and not being obliged to die, died for our sake; and for this reason, having abolished death, he has been raised, and has loosed our passions and the condemnation, the passionless one having suffered by his own act; and having become the author of our life, he was taken up into the heavens.

Καὶ οἱ ἐγόεδυμένοι 224ἡαεῖς αὐτὸν τῷ βαπτίσματι, ἀπ´ οὐρανοῦ αὐτὸν ἐκδεχόμεθα · ὅτε καὶ ἀναστήσει ἡμᾶς, καὶ τὴν ἀφθαρ-, σίαν τελέως χορηγήσει, ἦς τοὺς ἀῤῥαβῶνας νῦν διὰ τοῦ Θαπτίσματος δέδωκεν. Ὅμως καὶ ἐν παντὶ καιρῷ ἀναγκαῖόν ἐστι βαπτίζειν, μή ποτέ τι κώλυμα γένηται, καί τις ἀπέλθῃ ἁμύητος, τουτέστι χωρὶς βαπτίσματος. Καὶ πλείστη ἐκ τούτου ἔσται ζημία. Ὡ γὰρ πὴ θαπτισθεὶς οὐ σωθήσεται. Δέον δὲ ὅσον ἐγχωρεῖ ποιεῖν τὰ βαπτίσματα μετὰ λαμπρότητές τε καὶ εὐλαβείας. Τὸν ἱερέα τε χρῆ προσεκτικὸν καὶ νηφάλιον εἶναι. Τοῦ Θεοῦ γὰρ τὸ μέγιστον ἔργον διενεργεῖ, τὴν τῶν ἀνθρώπων ἁνάπλασιν. Ἀλλ´ ἐπὶ τὸ προκείμενον ἔλθωμεν. Τοῦ ἀρχιερέως προηγουμένου, προσάγει τὸν βαπτιζόμενον ὁ ἀνάδοχος τῇ Ἰερᾷ κολυμβήθρα· ὁ ἐγγυώμενος τὸν ἑκουσίως προσερχόμενον. Καὶ ἵσταται ὁ ἀρχιερεὺς, τὸν Χοιστὸυ αὐτὸν ἐκτυπῶν, τὴν αὐτοῦ δύναμιν ἔχων. Καὶ πληροῦται ἡ κολυμβήθρα ὕδατος, τὸν Ἰορδάνην τυποῦσα. Καὶ ἀνάπτονται φῶτα, τὸν φωτισμὸν δηλοῦντα τοῦ Πνεύματος· καὶ ὅτι ὁ βαπτιζόμενος ἐκ τοῦ σκότους ἔρχεται πρὸς τὸ φῶς, καὶ υἱὸς φωτὸς ἤδη γίνεται. Θυμιᾷ τε κύκλῳ τὴν κολυμβήθραν ὁ ἀρχιεΚρεὺς, τὴν εὐωδίαν καὶ ἀγιωσύνην δηλῶν τοῦ Πνεύματος, τοῦ πᾶσαν δυσωδίαν ἁμαρτημάτων ἐκπλύνοντος. ΑΠ Δγιον γὰρ τὸ Πνεῦμα. Εἶτα εὐλογεῖ καὶ ὑμνεῖ τὴν βασιλείαν τῆς ἁγίας Τριάδος τοῦ μόνου Θεοῦ ὁ ἀρχιερεύς. Μόνη γὰρ αὕτη βασιλεύει· καὶ ὅτι νῦν ἐβασίλευσεν, ἐκ τοῦ πονηροῦ ῥυσαμένη τὸι ἄρτι ἀναγεννώμενον. Εἶτα λεγομένων τῶν εἰρηνικῶν Κπροσευχῶν καὶ αἰτήσεων παρὰ τοῦ διακόνου, καὶ τοῦ λαοῦ τὸ,» Κύριε, ἐλέησον,» ἐκβοῶντος· ὄντως γὰρ ἐκεῖσε τὸ τοῦ Θεοῦ μέγα ἐπιβραβεύεται ἔλεος, υίοθετουμένου τοῦ ποὶν ἐχθροῦ τε καὶ ἐναγοῦς, ὁ ἀρχιερεὺς εἰς ἑαυτὸν εὔχεται ὑπὲρ ἑαυτοῦ, ζητων ἐκ Θεοῦ κάθαρσίν τε καὶ ἱλασμὸν εὑρεῖν, ἵνα μὴ ἔλλους καθαίρων, αὐτὸς μεμολυσμένος ταῖς ἁμαρσίαις εὐρεθῇ. Καὶ ἰσχὺν ἑξαιτεῖται καὶ χάριν, καλῶς τε καὶ ὡς πρέπον ἐστὶν ἐξυπηρετῆσαι τῷ μυστηρίῳ. Μέγα γὰρ τὸ μυστήριον, ἀγγέλων περιισταμένων, xριστοῦ μορφουμένου τοῖς ὕδασι, Πνεύματος ἀγίου ἐπιφοιτῶντος, καὶ τοῦ βαπτιζομένου ἀπὸ ῥερυπωμένου καὶ ἀμαρτωλοῦ καὶ ἀκαθάρτου καὶ ἐχθροῦ καὶ φθαρτοῦ ἁγίου γινομένου καὶ καθαροῦ καὶ υἱοῦ Θεοῦ καὶ ἁφθάρτου. Ὡ τῆς μεγάλης χάριτος! Σι τῆς δωρεᾶς τοῦ Θεοῦ! ὤ τῆς πρὸς ἡμᾶς εὐεργεσίας καὶ χάριτος! Διὸ καὶ πληρωθεισῶν τῶν αἰτήσεων, ἐξαναστὰε ὁ ἀρχιερεὺς ἀπὸ τῆς εὐχῆς, «Μέγας εἶ, Κύριε, ἐκβοᾷ, καὶ θαυμαστὰ τὰ ἔργα σου.» Καὶ τοῦτα ἀνακράζει τρὶς, τὸ τρισσὸν ἀνυμνῶν τῆς Θεότητος, καὶ μεγαλόνων τὸν εὐεργέτην.

Εἶτα πρὸς τὸν σαρκωθέντα ὑπὲρ ἡμῶν Υἱὸν τοῦ Θεοῦ τὴν εὐχὴν ἀναφέρων, καὶ τὰ ἀπαρχῆς τούτου πάντα καταλέγων θαυμάσια, τέλος καὶ τὴν σάρκωσιν εἰς ὑπόμνπσιν ἄγει τούτου τὴν ἐκ Παρθένου ἐν Πνεύματι, καὶ τὴ βάπτισμα ἐν τῷ Ἰορδάνη, δι´ οὖ ἡμᾶς ἀνέπλασε, συντρίψας τὰς κεφαλὰς τῶν δρακόντων ἐπὶ τοῦ ἵδατος. ὡς ὁ Προφήτης φησίν. Ὅθεν καὶ παρεῖναι αὐτὸν ἐκδυσωπεῖ διὰ τῆς ἐπιφοιτήσεως τοῦ ἁγίου Πνεύματος, καὶ ἀγιάσαι τὸ ὕδωρ.

And we who have put him on 224in baptism await him from heaven; when he will also raise us up, and supply the incorruption perfectly, whose earnests he has now given through baptism. Nevertheless at every season too it is necessary to baptize, lest at some time some hindrance arise, and someone depart uninitiated, that is, without baptism. And very great loss will come of this. For he who is not baptized will not be saved. And it is needful, as far as there is room, to perform the baptisms with splendour and reverence. And the priest must be attentive and sober. For he works through the greatest work of God, the refashioning of men. But let us come to the matter before us. The bishop going before, the sponsor brings the one being baptized to the sacred font; he who stands surety for the one who comes willingly. And the bishop stands, figuring forth Christ himself, having his power. And the font is filled with water, typifying the Jordan. And lights are lit, showing the illumination of the Spirit; and that the one being baptized comes out of the darkness to the light, and now becomes a son of light. And the bishop censes the font round about [the Latin gives Circum altare, around the altar], showing the fragrance and holiness of the Spirit, who washes out all the foul odour of sins. *81* For holy is the Spirit. Then the bishop blesses and hymns the kingdom of the holy Trinity, the only God. For it alone reigns; and because it has now reigned, having rescued from the evil one him who is just now being regenerated. Then, while the prayers of peace and the petitions are being said by the deacon, and the people are crying out «Lord, have mercy,» — for truly there the great mercy of God is awarded, as he who was before an enemy and accursed is adopted as a son — the bishop prays within himself for himself, seeking to find from God cleansing and propitiation, lest, while cleansing others, he himself be found defiled with sins. And he asks for strength and grace to minister to the mystery well and as is fitting. For great is the mystery: angels standing round, Christ being formed in the waters, the Holy Spirit coming upon them, and the one being baptized becoming, from filthy and sinful and unclean and an enemy and corruptible, holy and pure and a son of God and incorruptible. O the great grace! O the gift of God! O the beneficence and grace toward us! Wherefore also, the petitions being completed, the bishop, rising up from the prayer, «Great art thou, O Lord, he cries out, and wondrous are thy works.» And this he cries aloud three times, hymning the threeness of the Godhead, and magnifying the Benefactor.

Then, offering up the prayer to the Son of God who was made flesh for us, and recounting all his wonders from the first, at the end he also brings to remembrance his incarnation from the Virgin in the Spirit, and the baptism in the Jordan, through which he refashioned us, having crushed the heads of the dragons upon the water, as the Prophet says. Wherefore he also entreats him to be present through the coming of the Holy Spirit, and to sanctify the water.

Ἀχώριστος γὰρ ὁ Υἱὸς 225τοῦ Πατρὸς καὶ τοῦ Πνεύματος, καὶ κοινὴ ἡ χάρις αὐτοῖς. Καὶ σφραγίζει τρὶς τῇ χειρὶ σταυροειδῶς τὸ ὑδωρ, τὸν Χριστὸν ἐντυπῶν αὐτῷ. Καὶ ποιῆσαι αὐτὸ εὔχεται ἀφθαρσίας πηγὴν, καὶ τὰ λοιπά Εἶτα μετ´ ὀλίγον πάλιν ἐμφυσᾷ τῷ ὕδτι τρίτον, τὴν θείαν χάριν αὐτῷ δαψιλέστερον ἐντιθεὶς, μιμούμενος τὸν Χριστὸν, καὶ τὸ, «Πνεῦμα Θεοῦ ἐπεφέρετο ἐπάνω τοῦ ὕδατος,» διὰ τὸ βάπτισμα, ἐξαρχῆς εἶναι μαρτυρῶν. Καὶ σφραγίζει αὐτὸ πάλιν τῇ χειρὶ σταυρὸν ἐκτυπῶν, καί φησι, «Συντριβήτωσαν ὑπὸ τὴν σημείωσιν τοῦ τύπου τοῦ σταυροῦ οου πᾶσαι αἱ ἐναντίαι δυνάμεις.» Καὶ τὰ λοιπὰ δὲ λέγει, ὥστε πνει. ματικὸν εἶναι τὸ ὕδωρ καὶ ἅγιον, καὶ μηδὲν ὑποκρυβῆναι τούτῳ τοῦ πονηροῦ, ἀλλὰ μᾶλλον ἐναντίον αὐτῶ καὶ διωκτήριον εἶναι τοῦτο, καὶ ἀναπλάττειν καὶ ἀγιάζειν καὶ υἷοθετεῖν τοὺς βαπτιζομένους ἐν στούτῳ δυνάμενον, καὶ φωτὸς τέκνα ποιεῖν. Οὕτως οὖν ἑπευξάμενος, καὶ εἰρήνην δοὺς, ὅς ἐστιν Ἱησοῦς. Χριστὸς, πάρεστι γὰρ ἐκεῖσε ἡ ὑπερέχουσα πάντα νοῦν εἰρήνη Χριστὸς, καὶ ἀντιλαβών τὴν εἰρήνην ὁ ἱεράρχης, καὶ κλίνας τὴν κεφαλὴν, καθ´ ἑαυτὸν πἀλιν εὔχεται, ἐν ἡσυχίᾳ, τὸν εἰρηνικὸν καὶ ἡσύχιον καὶ πανταχοῦ προσκαλούμενος Ἰησουν, «Ἀὺ, λέγων, ὁ ἐν Ἰορδάνῃ παραγενόμενος καὶ ἀγιάσας τὰ ὕδατα, δέξαι καὶ ἡμᾶς δουλικῶς σοι προσπίπτοντας· καὶ ἁγίασον ταῦτα τὰ ὕδατα καὶ ἡμᾶς· καὶ Χριστὸς ἀνήγειρεν, ἀνυμνεῖ, ἐκφωνήσας τοῦτον ὡς πάντων ἁγιασμόν. Τότε τοίνυν πλήρης ἡ κολυμβηθρα τοῦ Πνεύματος, καὶ ἅγιον τὸ ὕδωρ, καὶ τὸν Χριστὸν φέρον ἀοράτως ἐν ἑαυτῷ. Ἐπεὶ δὲ καὶ τὸν τύπον τοῦ θείου ἐλέους παρεῖναι δεῖ, καὶ ἅγιον ἔλαιον ἐνεργεῖται. Τὸ μὲν γαρ ὕδωρ σημαίνει τὴν ἕκπλυσίν τε καὶ κάθαρσιν, καὶ ἤδη ἡγίασται· τὸ ἔλαιον δὲ τὸ θεῖον ἔλεος δείκνυσι, καὶ τὸ ἱλαρὸν αὐτοῦ τῆς χρηστότητος. Ὃ δὴ καὶ τοῖς τετραυματισμένοις παρὰ τοῦ κοινοῦ ἐχθροῦ καὶ μωλωπισθεῖσι Σαρέχεται, ὡς ἐν τοῖς Εὐαγγελίοις φησὶ Δ Χριστός. Τοῦτο καὶ ἐπὶ τοῦ Νῶε ἐδείχθη. Περιστερὰ γὰρ· ἦλθε τοῖς εἰς τὸ ὕδωρ ἔτι οὖσι μετὰ τῆς κιβωτοῦ, κάρφος ἐλαίας τῳ στόματι φέρουσα. Καὶ τὸ μὲν θδωρ τὸ βάπτισμα προετύπου, ἡ περιστερὰ δὲ τὸ Πνεῦμα τὸ ἄγιον· τὸ κάρφος δὲ τῆς ἐλαίας ὃ ἐν τῷ στόματι ἔφερεν, εἰς δεῖγμα τοῦ ἐλέους ἦν, καὶ τῆς ἀπείρου τοῦ Θεοῦ χρηστότητος. Ὃθεν καὶ ὧδε ἐν τῷ βαπτίσματι ἔλαιον προσάγεται ἅγιον. Ἀγιάζεται δὲ οὔτως· προσάγεται δι´ ἀγγείου τινὸς καθατύπω σταυροῦ τῆ χειρὶ, τῷ μὲν ἐμφυσήματι τὴν χάριν τοῦ Πνεύματος δηλῶν, ἣν ἔλαβεν ἀπὸ τοῦ Χριστοῦ τῷ ἐν τοῖς ἀποστόλοις θείῳ ἐμφυσήματι ὁ ἀρχιερεύς· τῷ σταυρῷ δὲ τυπῶν αὐτὸν τὸν σταυρωθέντα ὑπὲρ ἡμῶν, ὃς διὰ τοῦ σταυροῦ τὴν σωτηρίαν ἡμῖν ἐτέλεσε. Διὸ καὶ τελειοποιὸς αὐτὸς ὁ σταυΡὸς καὶ κωσὶς σταυροῦ οὐδὲν τελεῖται τῶν θείων. Εἶτα κλίνας τὴν κεφ αλήν, ἐπεύχεται ἁγιασθῆναι τὴ ελαιον τῷ ἁγίῳ Πνεύματι, ὑπ´ αὐτοῦ τοῦ καὶ ἐπὶ Νῶε τὸ κάρφος τῆς ἐλαίας διὰ περιστερᾶς ἀποστείλαντος, αὶ ἐν τῷ νόμῳ τοῦτο δεδωκότος τὸ ἔλαιον, καὶ τοῖς ἀποστόλοις παρεσχηκότος. Τελέσας δὲ τὴν εὐχὴν καὶ εὐχαριστήσας, ἐγχέει πρῶτον τῷ ὕδατι σταυροειδῶς τὸ ἁγιασθὲν ἅγιον τρίς, τὸ πολυύμνητον ξδων ᾷσμα, τὸ Ἀλληλούϊα. ΚΕΦΛΛ ἦΓ Τί σημαίνει τὸ Ἀλληλούῖα. Γοῦτο δὲ τὸ ἆσμα, τὸ Ἀλληλούτα, οἱ ἐμπνεόμενοι τῷ ἁγίῳ Πνεύματι ἔλεγον προφῆται. Ὃ δὴ ἐπιδημίαν Θεοῦ σημαίνει, καὶ τὸ, «Ἔρχεται ὁ Θεὸς, καὶ τοῦτον αἰνεῖτε,» προφητικῶς τεθὲν καὶ ἀδόμενον, διὰ τὸ μέλλειν τὸν Κύριον ἔρχεσθαι, καὶ κατὰ τὴν πρώτην αὐτοῦ καὶ τὴν δευτέραν παρουσίαν, καὶ τὴν χάριν αὐτοῦ ἀεὶ ἀποστέλλειν ἐν ἡμῖν ἐν ἀγίῳ Πνεύματι. Διὸ καὶ ἀεὶ τὸ ᾷσμα τοῦτο ἡ Ἐκκλησία μελῶν δεῖ, τὸν Χριστὸν λαβουσα, καὶ ἐξ οὐρανοῦ τοῦτοἐκδεχομένη, ὡς εἰρήκαμεν.

For the Son is inseparable 225from the Father and the Spirit, and the grace is common to them. And he seals the water three times with his hand in the form of a cross, imprinting Christ upon it. And he prays that he make it a fountain of incorruption, and the rest. Then after a little he again breathes upon the water three times, putting the divine grace into it more abundantly, imitating Christ, and bearing witness that the «The Spirit of God was borne upon the water,» was from the beginning on account of baptism. And he seals it again with his hand, figuring forth a cross, and says, «Let all the adverse powers be crushed beneath the sign of the figure of thy cross.» And he says the rest also, so that the water may be spiritual and holy, and that nothing of the evil one may lie hidden in it, but rather that it may be contrary to him and a thing that drives him away, and able to refashion and to sanctify and to adopt as sons those who are baptized in it, and to make them children of light. Having thus prayed over it, then, and having given peace, which is Jesus Christ — for there is present there the peace that passes all understanding, Christ — and the hierarch having received the peace in return, and having bowed his head, he prays again by himself, in stillness, calling upon Jesus, the peaceful and still, and that everywhere, «Thou, saying, who camest to the Jordan and didst sanctify the waters, receive us also, who fall before thee as servants; and sanctify these waters and us; and let them be for us unto health of soul and body.» And rising up, showing that Christ raised us up when we had fallen, he hymns him, having proclaimed him aloud as the sanctification of all. Then, therefore, the font is full of the Spirit, and the water is holy, and bears Christ invisibly in itself. And since the type of the divine mercy too must be present, holy oil also is brought into action. For the water signifies the washing-out and the cleansing, and has already been sanctified; but the oil shows the divine mercy, and the gladness of his kindness. Which indeed is also afforded to those who have been wounded by the common enemy and bruised, as in the Gospels *82* Christ says. This was shown in the case of Noah too. For a dove came to those who were still in the water with the ark, carrying a twig of olive in its mouth. And the water prefigured baptism, and the dove the Holy Spirit; and the twig of the olive which it carried in its mouth was for a token of the mercy, and of the boundless kindness of God. Whence here too in baptism holy oil is brought forward. And it is sanctified thus: it is brought in some clean vessel to the bishop, or to the priest, if the bishop is not present; and he breathes upon it. And he seals it three times with the figure of a cross with his hand, by the breathing showing the grace of the Spirit, which the bishop received from Christ by the divine breathing upon the apostles; and by the cross figuring him who was crucified for us, who through the cross accomplished salvation for us. Wherefore the cross itself is also perfecting, and without a cross none of the divine things is accomplished. Then, having bowed his head, he prays that the oil be sanctified by the Holy Spirit, by him who also in Noah's time sent the twig of the olive by a dove, and who gave this oil in the law, and afforded it to the apostles. And having completed the prayer and given thanks, he first pours the holy thing that was sanctified into the water three times in the form of a cross, singing the much-hymned song, the Alleluia.

CHAPTER 63. What the Alleluia signifies.

And this song, the Alleluia, the prophets who were inspired by the Holy Spirit used to say. Which indeed signifies a sojourning of God, and «God comes, and praise ye him,» having been set down and being sung prophetically, because the Lord was to come, both at his first and at his second coming, and to send his grace always among us in the Holy Spirit. Wherefore the Church also ever chants this song, having received Christ, and awaiting him from heaven, as we have said.

Ὁ μὲν οὖν θεῖος Διονυσιος ἐν τῇ ἰερᾷ κολυμβήθρᾳ μύρον ἐγχέεσθαι 228λέγει· νῦν δὲ τοῦτο ἐγχεῖται τὸ ἔλαιον, προοίμον ὃν τοῦ μύρου· τὸ μύρον δὲ ὑστερον. Ἔστι δὲ λογίσασθηι καὶ τὸν ἱερὸν Διονύσιον, τοῦτο τὸ ἔλαιον ὡς προοίμιον τοῦ μύρου μύρον καλεῖν, ὡς διὰ τούτου μὲν. τοῦ βαπτιζομένου ἀπαρχὴν λαμβάνοντος τοῦ ἁγιασμοῦ, καὶ πρὸς τοῦς θείους ἀγῶνας ἀλειφομένου, ὕστερον δὲ ἐνισχυομένου τελείως καὶ τελειουμένου καὶ σφραγιζομένου τῷ τελεωτάτῳ μύρῳ τῆς χάριτος. Διὸ καὶ τῶν δύο τούτων ἐν τῷ βαπτίσματι μέμνηται. Ὁ δὲ ἀρχιερεὺς μετὰ τὸν ἀγιάσαι τὸ ἔλαιον τὸν βαπτισθέντα τελειοῖ. Ἀλλὰ καὶ τίς ἦν χρεία τὸν βαπτιζόμενον ὕστερον μύοῳ χρίεσθαι, εἰ ἐν τῷ βαπτίσματι ἦν τὸ μύρον; Ἐκ τούτου οὖν τοῦ ἐλαίου χρίει πρῶτον αὐτὸς τὸν ἤδη μέλλοντα βαπτισθῆναι, «Χρίεται, λέγων, ὁ δεῖνα ἔλαιον ἀγαλλιάσεως εἰς τὸ ὄνομα τῆς Τριάδος.» Ἡ ἀγαλλίασις δὲ ὑπάρχει τοῦ Πνεύματος, σώζοντος ἡμᾶς ἀγαθῶς τε καὶ ἱλαρῶς. «Ἀπόδος μοι γὰρ τὴν κναλλίασιν τοῦ Σωτηρίου σου, ὁ Δαβὶδ ἀναβοᾷ, καὶ Πνεύματι ἡγεμονικῷ στήριξόν με.» Εἶτα ἀπὸ κεφαλῆς ὑπό τινος διακόνου ἢ ὑπηρέτου ὅλον τὸ σῶμα χρίεται. Πρὸς γὰρ τοὺς θείους ἀγῶνας ὀλοτελῶς εὐτρεπίζεται. Οὐδὲ γὰρ ἵνα ἀναπεπτωκὼς ᾖ, προσῆλθεν, ἀλλ´ ἵνα μιμῆται τὸν Χριστὸν, ὃν ἐνδύεται, καὶ κατὰ τοῦ πονηροῦ παλαίῃ τε καὶ ἀνδρίζηται. Καὶ οἱ ἀγωνιζόμενοι δὲ τῇ σαρκὶ ἐλαίῳ πάλαι ἠλείφοντα Τέσε ὡΚν εἰσάγεται γυμνὸς ὅλος εἰς τὸ ὕδωρ, αθὰ καὶ γεγέννηται, καὶ καθὰ πρῶτον πέπλασται. Ἀλλὰ πρῶτον μὲν εἶχε τὴν θείαν ἐκείνην στολὴν, καὶ γυμνὸς ὢν, οὐκ ᾐσχύνετο· νῦν δὲ γυμνὸς γενηθεὶς, ἀπὸ τῆς σαρκὸς φέρει τὰ τῆς αἰσχύνης τῆς παραβάσεως. Πλὴν εἰσελθὼν τὸ ὕδωρ τὸ ἅγιον, τὴν μέν αἰσχύνην ἀποῤῥίπτει τῆς ἁμαρτίας· τῆς ἀφθαρσίας δὲ τὴν δόξαν ἀπολαμβάνειβ τε καὶ τὸ ἔνδυμα. Λαβόμενος γὰρ αὐτοῦ ὁ ἱεράρχης ἢ ὁ ἱερεὺς τῆς κεφαλῆς ἐν ᾗ τὰ αἰσθητήρια πάντα καὶ τὸ ἡγεμονικὸν ἐνεργεῖ, καὶ καταδύσας εἰς τὸ ὕδωρ ἅπαξ, ἀναβοᾶ «Βαπτίζεται ὁ δοῦλος τοῦ Θεοῦ ὁ δεῖνα εἰς τὸ ὄνομα τοῦ Πατρός,» Καὶ εὐθὺς ἀναδύει τοῦτον. Εἶτα καταδύει πάλιν λέγων, «Καὶ τοῦ Υἱοῦ,» Καὶ πάλιν ἀναδύει Καὶ ἐκ τρίτου καταδύσας, φησὶ, «Καὶ τοῦ άγίου Πνεύματος.» Καὶ οὕτως ἀνάγει τοῦτον τετελεσμένον τῷ θειοτάτῳ βαπτίσματι, ἅμα μὲν καὶ διὰ τῆς ἁγίας Τριάδος αὐτὸν ἀναπλάττων καὶ τελειῶν, καὶ τὰς τρεῖς ὑποστάσεις ἀνακηρύττων ἐν μιᾷ τῇ Θεότητι, εἰς τὸ ὄνομα λέγων, ὃ δηλοῖ τὸ μοναδικὸν τῆς Θεότητος, εἶτα τοῦ Πατρὸς καὶ τοῦ ξῆγςαγῆοης,.ποπςποςολλα προσώπων, ἅμα δὲ διὰ τῶν τριῶν καταδύσεών τε καὶ ἀναδύσεων τὴν τριήμερον ταφὴν καὶ τὴν ἁνάστασιν αἰνιττομενος τοῦ ὑπὲρ ἡμῶν σαρκὶ σταυρωθέντος καὶ ἀνασιάντος, ΚΕΦΑΛ. ΞΔʹ. Ὅτι τὸ, «Βαπτίεται ὁ δοῦλος τοῦ Θεοῦ,» τοῖ Οατίζω ἐχ ὼ τὸν δυῦλον τοῦ Θεοῦ,» διάφέρει.

Ἐαπτίζεται δέ, φησὶ, καὶ οὐ, Βαπτίζω ἐγὼ, ὡς οἱ Λατῖνοι κἀν τούτῳ καινοτομοῦντες, τὸ ἑκούσιον τοῦ βαπτιζομένου μαρτυρῶν ὁ ἀρχιερεύς. Τὸ γὰρ, Βαπτίζω ἐγὼ, οὐκ ἐμφαίνει τὸ ἑκουσίως βαπτίδεσθοι τὸν φωτιζόμενον. Ἐνδεχόμενον γὰρ καὶ κατὰ δυμαστείαν τινα, καὶ παρὰ γνωμην, καὶ αὐτὸν βαπτίζειν ΑΡΟΤ ΧΙν.

Now the divine Dionysius says that myron is poured into the sacred font 228; but now this oil is poured in, being a prelude of the myron; and the myron afterwards. And it is possible to reckon that the sacred Dionysius too calls this oil myron as being a prelude of the myron, since through it the one being baptized receives the first-fruits of sanctification, and is anointed for the divine contests, and afterwards is strengthened perfectly and perfected and sealed with the most perfect myron of grace. Wherefore he also makes mention of these two in baptism. And the bishop, after sanctifying the oil, perfects the one who has been baptized. But what need also was there for the one being baptized to be anointed afterwards with myron, if the myron was in the baptism? From this oil, then, he himself first anoints the one who is now about to be baptized, saying, «N. is anointed with the oil of gladness in the name of the Trinity.» And the gladness is of the Spirit, who saves us with goodness and with cheer. «For restore unto me the gladness of thy Salvation, David cries out, and establish me with a governing Spirit.» Then from the head the whole body is anointed by some deacon or attendant. For he is made ready entirely for the divine contests. For he has not come forward to lie back at ease, but to imitate Christ, whom he puts on, and to wrestle against the evil one and to play the man. And those who contend in the flesh, too, were of old anointed with oil. Then, therefore, he is brought into the water wholly naked, just as he was also born, and as he was first fashioned. But at first he had that divine robe, and being naked, was not ashamed; but now, having become naked, he bears from the flesh the marks of the shame of the transgression. Yet having entered the holy water, he casts off the shame of sin; and receives back the glory *83* and the garment of incorruption. For the hierarch, or the priest, taking hold of his head, in which all the senses and the governing faculty operate, and plunging him into the water once, cries out, «The servant of God N. is baptized in the name of the Father,» And at once he brings him up. Then he plunges him again, saying, «And of the Son,» And again he brings him up. And having plunged him a third time, he says, «And of the Holy Spirit.» And thus he leads him up, initiated by the most divine baptism, at once both refashioning and perfecting him through the holy Trinity, and proclaiming the three hypostases in the one Godhead, saying "in the name," which shows the singleness of the Godhead, then "of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit," which is the confession of the three divine persons, undivided and unconfused; and at the same time, through the three plungings and risings, hinting at the three days' burial and the resurrection of him who was crucified in the flesh for us and rose.

CHAPTER 64. That «The servant of God is baptized,» differs from «I baptize the servant of God.»

And Is baptized, he says, and not, I baptize, as the Latins, innovating even in this — the bishop bearing witness to the willingness of the one being baptized. For the I baptize does not make plain that the one being illumined is baptized willingly.

ΚοοῦκορΟΟΘ 229ἀφ´ ἑαυτοῦ τὸν βαπτίζοντα μόνον, Τὸ δὲ,«Παπτίθ ζεται, » καὶ τὸ ἑκουσίως σημαίνει, καὶ παρ´ ἐμοῦ τοῦ ἀρχιερέως ἢ ἱερέως, καὶ τὸ ἔργον ἤδη ποιοῦντος νὶ τοῦ ἐν Τριάδι μόνου Θεοῦ, οὖ καὶ τὸ ὄνομᾳ τῷ βαπτιζομένῳ τρανῶς ἐπιλέγεται. Οὕτως οὖν ἀναγεννηθεὶς ὁ βαπτισθεὶς, ἐξέρχεται καινὸς ὅλος καὶ πεφωτισμένος καὶ Υίὸς Θεοῦ τῆς ἱερᾶς κολυμβήθρας τῆς μητρὸς ἡμῶν τῆς πνευματικῆς, τῆς ἀντὶ τῆς παρθενικῆς καθαρᾶς καὶ ἀγίσς μήτρας. Ὡς γὰρ ὁ Χριστὸς ἀπὸ τῆς Παρθένου, ἵνα τὸν ῥερυπωμένον καθάρῃ τόκον, καὶ ἡμεῖς ἀπὸ τῆς ἁγνῆς κολυμβήθρας, καὶ ὡς ἐκεῖ αἷμα καθαρὸν καὶ γαστὴρ ἀγία τῆς Παρθένου ἐν ἁγίῳ Πνεύματι τὴν τοῦ Λόγου εἰργάσατο σάρκωσιν, καὶ ἐν ἡμῖν ὕδωρ καθαρὸν τῆς κλυμβήθρας καὶ Πνεῦμα θεῖον τὴν καθαρὰν ἀναγέννησιν ἐξετέλεσε. Πλὴν ὥσπερ ἐκεῖσε ἐν τῷ τοῦ Σατῆοος βαπτίσματι καὶ Πνεύματος κάθοδος ἦν, ἵνα δείξῃ ὅλον τὸ πλήρωμα ἐν τῷ σαρκωθέντι σωματικῶς τῆς Θεότητος, καὶ ἐν ἡμῖν τὸ χρίσμα τοὺ μύρου μετὰ τὸ βάπτισμα, ἵνα καὶ ἡμεῖς λάβωμεν τὴν χάθν τοῦ Πνεύματος. «Ἐκ τοῦ πληρώματος αὐτοῦ γὰρ, φησὶ, καὶ ἡμεῖς πάντες ἐλάβομεν, καὶ χάριν ἀντὶ χάριτος.ν ΚΕΦΙΛ. ΞΕʹ. πσαγ έχει τῷ βαπτιζομένῳ τὸ χρίσμα, ἢοι » χιύρον. Διὸ καὶ τῷ θείῳ τοῦτον παρευθὺς χρίει μύρῳ, Στερ οὐκ ἔλαιον μόνον ἐστὶν, ἀλλὰ καὶ ἐκ πλείστων ἄλλων εἰδῶν εὐόσμων συγικείμενον, παριστάνον καὶ ἐν συμβόλοις τὸ πολυδύναμον, καὶ τὸ τῶν ἐνεργειῶν παικίλον τε καὶ πολυειδὲς τῶν χαρισμάτων τοῦ Πνεύματος, καὶ τῆς αὐτοῦ ἁγιωσύνης τὸ εὔοσμον. Δἰδοται οὖν τοῦτο ἡμῖν, καὶ ὡς σφραγὶς Χριστοῦ καὶ σημεῖον, ἐπεὶ καὶ Χριστὸς δι´ αὐτὸ τοῦτο κατονομάζεται, ὅτι τὴν τοῦ Πνεύματος ὅλην δύναμιν καὶ σωματικῶς εἶχεν ἐν ἑαῦτῷ παρὰ τοῦ Πατρός. Καὶ Ἡσαΐας τοῦτό φησι, «Πνεῦμα Κυοίου ἐπ´ ἐμὲ, οὗ εῖνεκεν ἔχρισέ με.» Καὶ ἡμεῖς δὲ ἐξ αὐτοῦ τὴν χάριν λαβόντες ἐν τῷ μύρῳ, Χριστιανοὶ καλούμεθα, καὶ χριστοὶ Κυρίου ἐσμέν· οὐ γὰρ ἀπαξ οἱ κατ´ αὐτὸν καλεῖσθαι ἡμᾶς· ὅτι καὶ υἱοὺς Θεοῦ θέσει καλεῖ ὁ κατὰ φύσιν Υἱὸς καὶ θεοὺς κατὰ χάριν ὁ φύσει καὶ Χριστοὺς ἐν τῇ χάριτι ὁ κατὰ φύσιν ἔχων τὴνχάριν ἐν έαυτῶ ὡς ἦό τοῦ Θεοῦ Πατρὸς Λόγος καὶ ἀδιαίρετος πν τῶ ἱνεύματι. Χρίων οὖν τὸν βαπτισθέντα ἤδη ὁ ὰρχιερεὺς οὕτω φησί· «Σφραγίς δωρεᾶς Πνεύματος ἁγίου. Ἀμήν.» Ἐκ τούτου δηλῶν, ὡς σημεῖόν ἓστι τοῦ Χριστοῦ τὸ χρίσμα, ἐπεὶ καὶ σταυροτύπως χρίεται ὁ χριόμενος, καὶ τὴν δωρεὰν χορηγεῖ τοῦ ΚΕΦΙΛ. ϛʹ. Οτι δυνάμει πᾶς βαπτισθεὶς καὶ χρισθεὶς ἐν ἕαυτῷ ἔχει τὰ θεῖα χαρίσματα. κατὰ καιρων ὰποκαλυπτόμενα, πλὴν τῆς ἱερωσύνης. Λονάαει γὰρ ὁ βαπτισθεὶς καὶ χρισθεὶς ἐν ἑαυτῷ ἔχει τὰ θεῖα χαρίσματα· καὶ ἀναλόγως ἀποκαλύπτεται τούτω ταῦτα κατὰ καιοὸν καθόσον ἑαυ-ὶν ἐκκα θάρη, καὶ καθόσον ἄξιον ἑαυτὸν τοῦ ἐνεργ.ῖν ταῦτα εν αὐτῷ ἀποδείξῃ, πλὴν τῆς θείας μόνης ἰερωσύνης.

For it is possible, even by some compulsion and against his will, for the one baptizing to baptize him 229of himself alone. But the «Is baptized,» signifies both the willingness, and that it is by me, the bishop or priest, who am already doing the work, and by the only God in Trinity, whose name too is clearly pronounced over the one being baptized. Thus, then, regenerated, the one baptized comes out wholly new and illumined and a son of God from the sacred font, our spiritual mother, which stands in place of the virginal pure and holy womb. For as Christ came from the Virgin, that he might cleanse the defiled birth, so we too from the pure font; and as there the pure blood and the holy belly of the Virgin in the Holy Spirit wrought the incarnation of the Word, so in us also the pure water of the font and the divine Spirit have accomplished the pure regeneration. Only, just as there, at the baptism of the Saviour, there was also a descent of the Spirit, that he might show the whole fullness of the Godhead bodily in him who was made flesh, so in us also there is the chrism of the myron after the baptism, that we too may receive the grace of the Spirit. «For of his fullness, he says, have all we too received, and grace for grace.»

CHAPTER 65. What the chrism, that is, the myron, affords to the one being baptized.

Wherefore he also anoints him straightway with the divine myron, which is not oil alone, but is also compounded of very many other fragrant kinds, presenting in symbols too the manifold power, and the variety and multiformity of the operations of the gifts of the Spirit, and the fragrance of his holiness. This, then, is given to us also as a seal and sign of Christ, since Christ too is named for this very reason [the Latin gives quoniam Christus ideo dixit se … habere, since Christ therefore said that he had], that he had in himself, bodily also, the whole power of the Spirit from the Father. And Isaiah says this: «The Spirit of the Lord is upon me, because he hath anointed me.» And we too, having received the grace from him in the myron, are called Christians, and are christs of the Lord; for he does not disdain that we be called after him; because he who is Son by nature calls us sons of God by adoption, and he who is God by nature calls us gods by grace, and he who by nature has the grace in himself calls us christs in grace, as being *84* the Word of God the Father and undivided from the Spirit. Anointing the one now baptized, then, the bishop says thus: «The seal of the gift of the Holy Spirit. Amen.» Showing by this that the chrism is a sign of Christ, since the one anointed is also anointed in the figure of a cross, and that it supplies the gift of the Holy Spirit.

CHAPTER 66. That potentially every one baptized and anointed has in himself the divine gifts, revealed in due season, except the priesthood.

For potentially the one baptized and anointed has in himself the divine gifts; and these are revealed to him proportionately in due season [the Latin gives ea secundum tempus abscondit, he hides them according to the time], insofar as he cleanses himself, and insofar as he shows himself worthy of their operating in him, except the divine priesthood alone.

Ἀὕτη γὰρ ὡς δῶρον ἴδιον, καὶ μόνον ἔργον Θεοῦ, τῇ 232χειροτονίᾳ χαρίζεται. Καὶ οὐδεὶς ἐνεργήσει τι τῶν ἰερῶν, εἰ καὶ σημειοφόρος, χωρὶς τῆς χειροτονίας. Ἰάματα δὲ ποιεῖν καὶ προφητεύειν καὶ καθαρεύειν σώματί τε καὶ λογισμοῖς, καὶ ἀληθῶς παρθενεύειν, καὶ προσεύχεσθαι διηνεκῶς τε καὶ ἀῤῥειβάστως καὶ πρὸς ἀγάπην θείαν θερμαίνεσθαι καὶ κινεῖσθαι, καὶ θείων ἐλλάμψεων ἀξιοῦσθαι, καὶ ἀποκαλύψεων Ιερῶν καὶ μυστηρίων καὶ θεωριῶν οὐρανίων, καὶ τῶν τοιούτων καὶ κατ´ ἀγγέλους σχεδὸν γενέσθαι, ὡς καὶ πλεῖστοι γεγόνασι, καὶ τῆς χειροτονίας χωρίς πολλοὶ διὰ τῆς χάριτος ἀπὸ τοῦ βαπτίσματος καὶ τοῦ μύρου, δύναμιν ἔσχον τυχεῖν, καὶ τυγχάνουσι, καὶ τῶν τοιούτων καταξιοῦνται. Καὶ ταῦτα μὲν περὶ τούτου. Πρὸ δέ γε τοῦ χρίσματος μετὰ τὸ βάπτισμα ὁ ἀρχιερεὺς, ἐνδυομένου τοῦ βαπτιζομένου χιτῶνα, ὃς εὐλογεῖται παρ´ αὐτοῦ, τὰ τῆς εὐχαριστίας πρὸς Θεὸν ἀναφέρει, ἀνυμνῶν αὐτὸν ὑπὲρ τῆς σωτηρίας τοῦ ἀναγεννηθέντος τῷ ὕδατι καὶ τῷ Πνεύματι. Εἶτα καὶ τοῦ χρίσματος ἀξιωθῆναι ἐκδυσωπεῖ, καὶ τῆς δωρεᾶς δι´ αὐτοῦ καὶ τῆς ἁγιωσύνης τοῦ θείου Πνεύματος. Ἄγ ος γὰρ ἀναδείκνυται καὶ ἐσφραγισμένος Χριστῷ, καὶ ἐπιγνωστὸς τοῖς ἀγγέλοις τῷ θείῳ μύρῳ, καὶ τοῖς ἐχθροῖς οὐχ ἀλώσιμος, εἰ μόνον γρηγορῶν εἴη. Καὶ τῆς κοινωνίας δὲ σοῦτον των μυστηρίων ἐπιτυχεῖν ἐξαιτεῖται, καὶ μὴ τέκνον ἕτι σώματος εἶναι, ἀλλὰ τέκνον τῆς τοῦ Θεοῦ βασιΚΕΑΛ. ΞΖʹ. Ἀιατί ἡ κουρὰ ἐν τοῖς βαπτιζομένοις τελεῖτσι καὶ τὸ ἔνδυμα τοῦ κουκουλίου καὶ τοῦ ἀναβόλου. Μετὰ τὸ χρίσμα, τὸ πανάγιόν φημι μύρον, κείρει τὰς τῆς κεφαλῆς αὐτοῦ σταυροειδῶς τρίχας, ἄμα μὲν καὶ διὰ τὸ κεφαλὴν ἔχειν τὸν Χριστὸν, καὶ ἀπιρικαλύπτως ὀφείλειν προσεύχεσθαι, ὡς καὶ ὁ Παῦλο διδάσκει, ἅμα δὲ καὶ ὡς σφραγῖὸά τινα τοῦτο ποιῶν τὴν κουρὰν, ἐπεὶ καὶ σταυροειδῶς τοῦτον κείρει, ἵνα, καὶ πᾶν περιττὸν τῶν ἐννοιῶν ἀποῤῥίπτη. Διὸ δὴ καὶ οἱ μοναχοὶ ἀποκείρονται. Καὶ πᾶς πιστὸς τὰ περιττὰ καὶ μὴ τοσοῦτον εἰς χρῆσιν παραιτεῖσθαι ὸφείλει. Ἔτι δὲ καὶ ὡς ἀπαρχὴ καὶ θυσία τοῦ ἀνθρωπείου σώματος ἀπὸ τοῦ βαπτισθέντος αἱ τρίχες Χριστῷ προσφέρονται. Τοῦ γὰρ ὅλου σώματος ὡς ἀναθυμίασίς εἰσι. Διὸ καὶ ὁ ἱεράρχης ταύτας λαβὼν οὐχ ὡς ἔτυχε ῥίπτει, ἀλλ´ ἐν τόπῳ ἱερῷ τίθησιν.

For this, as a gift of its own and the work of God alone, is bestowed by 232ordination. And no one will perform any of the sacred things, even if he be a wonder-bearer, without ordination. But to work healings and to prophesy and to be pure in body and in thoughts, and truly to live as a virgin, and to pray unceasingly and undistractedly, and to be warmed and moved toward divine love, and to be deemed worthy of divine illuminations, and of sacred revelations and mysteries and heavenly contemplations, and of such things, and to become almost like the angels — as very many have become, many even without ordination have had the power to attain through the grace from baptism and from the myron, and do attain, and are deemed worthy of such things. And so much concerning this. And before the chrism, after the baptism, the bishop, while the one baptized is putting on a tunic, which is blessed by him, offers up the thanksgiving to God, hymning him for the salvation of him who has been regenerated by the water and the Spirit. Then he also entreats that he be deemed worthy of the chrism, and of the gift through it and of the holiness of the divine Spirit. For he is shown forth holy and sealed to Christ, and recognizable to the angels by the divine myron, and not to be captured by the enemies [the Latin gives jam non nisi aegre inimicis expugnabilis, not to be taken by the enemies except with difficulty], if only he be watchful. And he asks also that he may attain to the communion of the mysteries, and no longer be a child of the body, but a child of the kingdom of God.

CHAPTER 67. Why the tonsure is performed on those being baptized, and the garment of the cowl and of the mantle.

After the chrism — the all-holy myron, I mean — he cuts the hairs of his head in the form of a cross, at once because he has Christ for head, and ought to pray uncovered, as Paul also teaches, and at once also making this tonsure as a kind of seal, since he also cuts him in the form of a cross, so that he may cast off everything superfluous of the thoughts as well. Wherefore indeed the monks too are tonsured. And every believer ought to renounce the superfluous things and those not so much for use. And further, as first-fruits and sacrifice of the human body, the hairs from the one baptized are offered to Christ. For they are as an exhalation of the whole body. Wherefore also the hierarch, taking them, does not throw them anywhere at random, but places them in a sacred place. Thus, then, the one baptized, having put on a tunic and been anointed, and been tonsured, and having received on his head a cowl, which indeed is also called a helmet — on the one hand on account of guilelessness and what is still childlike and pure, on the other for the shelter that is from God and the guarding of the senses, for most of the senses are seated in the head — then having worn over it a white tunic too, which is also called a mantle, for a type of the divine light, and of the angelic *85* purity — for the one baptized is a son of light, and has been shown wholly spotless and pure — thus initiated, he stands.
